# Copy this file to .env (docker compose reads it automatically) and paste your key below.
# Turns on the AI triage agent, the plain-English rules assistant and AI explanations.
# Everything else, including the ML drift model, works without it. Only aggregate statistics are ever sent
# to the Anthropic API, never rows. Get a key at https://console.anthropic.com/
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=

# Only if your key is NOT tied to a workspace (the API then answers "must include the anthropic-workspace-id
# header"): paste the workspace ID (starts with wrkspc_) from console.anthropic.com -> Settings -> Workspaces.
# Simpler alternative: create the API key inside a workspace and leave this empty.
ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID=

FILE [4/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\action.yml

name: "DataPulse data quality check"
description: "Language-agnostic data checks for any repository: compare a changed CSV/Parquet file with the baseline from the target branch, comment on the pull request, and fail CI on critical violations."
author: "DataPulse"
branding:
  icon: activity
  color: blue

inputs:
  data-file:
    description: "Path to the CSV or Parquet file to check (relative to working-directory)."
    required: true
  config:
    description: "Path to datapulse.yml (relative to working-directory)."
    required: false
    default: "datapulse.yml"
  baseline:
    description: "Baseline file path. Defaults to the `baseline` setting in the config."
    required: false
    default: ""
  baseline-ref:
    description: "Git ref to read the baseline from. Defaults to origin/<base branch> on pull requests."
    required: false
    default: ""
  working-directory:
    description: "Directory to run in, e.g. a service folder in a monorepo. Your project's language and files are irrelevant."
    required: false
    default: "."
  setup-python:
    description: "Install Python via actions/setup-python (true) or use the runner's existing python3 (false). DataPulse always installs into its own virtualenv and never touches your project's dependencies."
    required: false
    default: "true"
  binary-url:
    description: "URL of a standalone DataPulse executable (from the project's Releases). When set, Python is not installed or used at all."
    required: false
    default: ""
  binary-sha256:
    description: "Expected SHA-256 of the binary. The step fails on mismatch; strongly recommended with binary-url."
    required: false
    default: ""
  python-version:
    description: "Python version used to run DataPulse when setup-python is true."
    required: false
    default: "3.11"
  comment:
    description: "Post or update a pull request comment with the report (true/false)."
    required: false
    default: "true"
  discover:
    description: "Find code (Python, JS/TS, Java, Go, Rust, SQL, ...) that references the affected columns and show it in the report (true/false). Heuristic text search."
    required: false
    default: "false"
  config-from-base:
    description: "On pull requests, also read datapulse.yml from the target branch (true/false) so a PR cannot loosen its own rules. Rule changes in a PR take effect after it merges."
    required: false
    default: "true"
  ai:
    description: "Add AI explanations (true/false). Needs an API key in the step's env; only aggregate statistics are sent."
    required: false
    default: "false"
  github-token:
    description: "Token used to post the PR comment. Needs pull-requests: write."
    required: false
    default: ${{ github.token }}

outputs:
  exit-code:
    description: "0 when the check passed, 1 when issues reached the configured fail_on severity."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.exit-code }}
  status:
    description: "passed, failed, or error (the check could not run, e.g. missing baseline)."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.status }}
  issue-count:
    description: "Number of issues found."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.issue-count }}
  schema-changed:
    description: "true when columns were added, removed, or changed type."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.schema-changed }}
  affected-consumers:
    description: "Comma-separated downstream consumers mapped to issues."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.affected-consumers }}
  report-markdown:
    description: "Path to the Markdown report on the runner."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.report-markdown }}
  report-json:
    description: "Path to the machine-readable JSON report on the runner."
    value: ${{ steps.check.outputs.report-json }}

runs:
  using: composite
  steps:
    - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
      if: inputs.setup-python == 'true' && inputs.binary-url == ''
      with:
        python-version: ${{ inputs.python-version }}

    - name: Install DataPulse
      shell: bash
      env:
        DP_ACTION_PATH: ${{ github.action_path }}
        DP_BINARY_URL: ${{ inputs.binary-url }}
        DP_BINARY_SHA256: ${{ inputs.binary-sha256 }}
      run: |
        set -eu
        if [ -n "$DP_BINARY_URL" ]; then
          dir="$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-bin"; mkdir -p "$dir"
          name=datapulse; if [ "${RUNNER_OS:-}" = "Windows" ]; then name=datapulse.exe; fi
          curl --fail --silent --show-error --location "$DP_BINARY_URL" --output "$dir/$name"
          if [ -n "$DP_BINARY_SHA256" ]; then
            if command -v sha256sum >/dev/null 2>&1; then actual="$(sha256sum "$dir/$name" | cut -d' ' -f1)"; else actual="$(shasum -a 256 "$dir/$name" | cut -d' ' -f1)"; fi
            if [ "$actual" != "$DP_BINARY_SHA256" ]; then echo "SHA-256 mismatch for the downloaded DataPulse binary." >&2; exit 1; fi
          fi
          chmod +x "$dir/$name"
          echo "$dir" >> "$GITHUB_PATH"
          exit 0
        fi
        py="$(command -v python || command -v python3)"
        "$py" -m venv "$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-venv"
        if [ -d "$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-venv/Scripts" ]; then bin="$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-venv/Scripts"; else bin="$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-venv/bin"; fi
        "$bin/python" -m pip install --quiet --disable-pip-version-check "$DP_ACTION_PATH"
        echo "$bin" >> "$GITHUB_PATH"

    - name: Run DataPulse checks
      id: check
      shell: bash
      working-directory: ${{ inputs.working-directory }}
      env:
        DP_DATA_FILE: ${{ inputs.data-file }}
        DP_CONFIG: ${{ inputs.config }}
        DP_BASELINE: ${{ inputs.baseline }}
        DP_BASELINE_REF: ${{ inputs.baseline-ref }}
        DP_AI: ${{ inputs.ai }}
        DP_DISCOVER: ${{ inputs.discover }}
        DP_CONFIG_FROM_BASE: ${{ inputs.config-from-base }}
        DP_BASE_REF: ${{ github.base_ref }}
      run: |
        set +e
        md="$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-report.md"
        js="$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-report.json"
        rm -f "$md" "$js"   # never publish a stale report from an earlier step
        args=("$DP_DATA_FILE" --markdown "$md" --json "$js")
        if [ -n "$DP_BASELINE" ]; then args+=(--baseline "$DP_BASELINE"); fi
        ref="$DP_BASELINE_REF"
        if [ -z "$ref" ] && [ -n "$DP_BASE_REF" ]; then
          git fetch --no-tags --depth=1 origin "$DP_BASE_REF"
          ref="origin/$DP_BASE_REF"
        fi
        if [ -n "$ref" ]; then
          args+=(--baseline-ref "$ref")
          # Always read the rules from the trusted ref, even if the PR deleted or moved its config file.
          if [ "$DP_CONFIG_FROM_BASE" = "true" ]; then args+=(--config "$DP_CONFIG" --config-ref "$ref")
          elif [ -f "$DP_CONFIG" ]; then args+=(--config "$DP_CONFIG"); fi
        elif [ -f "$DP_CONFIG" ]; then
          args+=(--config "$DP_CONFIG")
        fi
        if [ "$DP_AI" = "true" ]; then args+=(--ai); fi
        if [ "$DP_DISCOVER" = "true" ]; then args+=(--discover); fi
        datapulse check "${args[@]}"
        code=$?
        {
          echo "exit-code=$code"
          echo "report-markdown=$md"
          echo "report-json=$js"
        } >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
        if [ -f "$js" ]; then datapulse github-outputs "$js" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"; else echo "status=error" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"; fi
        exit 0

    - name: Publish pull request comment
      if: always() && inputs.comment == 'true' && github.event_name == 'pull_request'
      shell: bash
      continue-on-error: true
      env:
        GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ inputs.github-token }}
      run: datapulse comment "$RUNNER_TEMP/datapulse-report.md"

    - name: Fail on violations
      if: always() && steps.check.outputs.exit-code != '' && steps.check.outputs.exit-code != '0'
      shell: bash
      env:
        DP_EXIT: ${{ steps.check.outputs.exit-code }}
      run: |
        echo "DataPulse found issues at or above the configured fail_on severity (exit code $DP_EXIT)."
        exit "$DP_EXIT"

# Dashboard + API:  docker compose up --build   ->  http://localhost:8080
services:
  api:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.api
    environment:
      DATAPULSE_HOME: /data
      ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}
      ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID: ${ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID:-}
      DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES: ${DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES:-}
    volumes:
      - datapulse-data:/data
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    restart: unless-stopped

  web:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.web
    ports:
      - "8080:80"
    depends_on:
      - api
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  datapulse-data:

# Run DataPulse without installing Python or anything else on your machine:
#   docker build -t datapulse .
#   docker run --rm --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -v "$PWD":/work datapulse check data/orders.csv
FROM python:3.12-slim

RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends git \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
 && git config --system --add safe.directory '*'

COPY pyproject.toml README.md /src/
COPY src /src/src
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir /src && rm -rf /src

WORKDIR /work
ENTRYPOINT ["datapulse"]
CMD ["--help"]

# DataPulse web API (the dashboard's backend). Started by docker-compose.yml.
FROM python:3.12-slim
ENV PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 DATAPULSE_HOME=/data
WORKDIR /app
COPY pyproject.toml README.md ./
COPY src ./src
COPY server ./server
COPY examples ./examples
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir '.[web,sources]'
EXPOSE 8000
CMD ["uvicorn", "server.app:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]

# DataPulse in one container: the dashboard and the API on one port, data on a volume at /data.
# For cloud hosts (Railway, Fly.io, Render...). Local development can keep using docker-compose.yml.
FROM node:24-alpine AS web
WORKDIR /web
COPY web/package.json ./
RUN npm install
COPY web ./
RUN npm run build

FROM python:3.12-slim
ENV PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 DATAPULSE_HOME=/data DATAPULSE_WEB_DIR=/app/web
WORKDIR /app
COPY pyproject.toml README.md ./
COPY src ./src
COPY server ./server
COPY examples ./examples
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir '.[web,sources]'
COPY --from=web /web/dist ./web
EXPOSE 8000
# Hosts set $PORT; they also terminate HTTPS in front of the app, hence --proxy-headers.
CMD ["sh", "-c", "exec uvicorn server.app:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT:-8000} --proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips='*'"]

FILE [13/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\README.md

# DataPulse

An AI data-quality engineer for your pipelines. DataPulse catches data changes that would break dashboards,
services or ML models, then does the on-call work: it investigates, says whether to block the release, ranks the
likely causes, writes the fix checklist and drafts the message to the people affected.

- **Rules** (schema, nulls, row counts, duplicates, categories, distributions) run offline and deterministically.
- **An ML drift model** learns to tell new rows from trusted rows and names the columns that changed, including
  shifts in how columns relate to each other that no single-column rule can see. Runs locally, no API key.
- **An AI triage agent** (Claude, with tools) investigates each failure and writes the incident note.
- **A rules assistant** turns "order_id must be unique and alert if amount shifts" into a validated `datapulse.yml`.

## AI and ML features

| Feature | What it takes off your plate | Where | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| ML drift model (`ml_drift` check) | Spotting subtle or multi-column drift and pointing at the columns responsible | Dashboard (automatic), `check --reference good.csv` | nothing; runs locally |
| Investigate with AI | One button, two agents in parallel. The triage agent handles the first hour of an incident (root cause, block-or-ship call, fix steps, who to tell, the message); the repair agent fixes a copy (rescales units, converts text back to numbers, removes duplicates, standardizes spellings) and re-runs every check to prove it worked | Dashboard **Investigate with AI**, `check --triage`, `datapulse fix data.csv -o fixed.csv` | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |
| AI source mapping | Converting each new data source to your standard format: renamed columns, other units, numbers stored as text, different spellings. Approved once, then replayed on every load without AI | Dashboard **Sources** tab | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (name matching works without it) |
| Before/after charts | Seeing a shift instantly: each column's value ranges and empty rates, baseline vs. new file | Dashboard, on each issue | nothing |
| Rules assistant | Writing and debugging YAML; drafts are validated and self-corrected before you see them | Dashboard Rules tab, `datapulse draft-rules "..."` | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |
| Issue explanations | A hedged hypothesis per issue | `check --ai`, dashboard toggle | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |

**How the drift model works.** It samples up to `max_rows` rows from each file and trains a random forest to
predict which file a row came from. Its out-of-bag ROC AUC is the drift score: 0.5 means the files are
indistinguishable, 1.0 means completely different. Above `max_auc` (default 0.7) it reports an issue naming the
columns the model relied on (feature importance), and those columns map to your downstream consumers like any
other issue. Identifier columns (all-unique values) and timestamps are skipped automatically, because they differ
between any two batches; add more to `checks.ml_drift.exclude`.

**How the triage agent works.** Claude gets read-only tools: `list_issues`, `get_column_stats` (baseline vs.
current aggregates), `get_run_history` and `get_consumers`. It decides what to look at, then returns a strict JSON
triage (verdict `block` / `investigate` / `safe_to_ship`, ranked hypotheses with confidence and evidence, actions
with owners, who to notify, a ready-to-send message). The dashboard shows the steps it took. Its output is labelled
AI-generated and never changes a check's pass/fail result.

**How the repair agent works.** It can only use a fixed set of safe repair operations (rename, rescale, convert to
numbers, remove duplicates, fill or drop empty values, drop a column, standardize spellings), never arbitrary code.
It applies them one at a time to a copy and re-checks after each, and it is told not to invent data (a missing
column or blank identifiers are left for a person). Its operations are logged and replayed onto the original to
produce the repaired file, then every check runs again, so "9 issues -> 5" is measured. The original is never
changed.

**Privacy.** Every AI feature sends column names, aggregate statistics and issue metrics only. Raw rows, category
values and example values are never sent; tests enforce this for each feature. The ML model never leaves your machine.

**Setting the key.** CLI: `export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...` (PowerShell: `$env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="..."`). Docker:
copy `.env.example` to `.env`, put your key in it, then `docker compose up --build`. The model is `ai.model` in
`datapulse.yml` (default `claude-sonnet-5-5`); `DATAPULSE_AI_URL` points the client at a proxy.

## Data sources and unified data

This is DataPulse's main job: the same data lives in many places, each in its own format, and DataPulse turns it
into one checked table. **Try it with sample data** creates a project with three sample databases (a warehouse, a
store system that keeps amounts in cents, and an online shop that stores prices as text) to map and unify.

The **Sources** tab (the first tab of every project) connects a project to where its data lives, so nobody
downloads and uploads files:

**Add source** offers three choices:

| Choice | What you give it |
|---|---|
| **Database** | A connection URL. DataPulse recognises the database from it: PostgreSQL (also Supabase, Neon, Railway, RDS, Redshift), MySQL / MariaDB, Microsoft SQL Server / Azure SQL, MongoDB (also Atlas, DocumentDB, Cosmos DB), and any other database with a SQLAlchemy driver installed (Snowflake, BigQuery, Oracle, Databricks…). Then a table name, one `SELECT` query, or for MongoDB a collection (`database.collection`). |
| **File** | An uploaded CSV, Excel, JSON or Parquet file, or a document (PDF, Word, text, Markdown, email, HTML, image). Documents are read once at upload: the AI unifier extracts their records into a table (without an API key, each document becomes one record of its text). Edit the source to upload a newer version. |
| **Google Drive** | A file shared as "Anyone with the link can view": data files and documents alike. |
| **Link** | A public `https://` link to a data file or document, including Google Sheets share links. |
| **REST API** | Any JSON API URL, with optional headers (kept on the server) and the path to the rows (found automatically if empty). Nested fields become columns. |
| **Web page** | A public page: its address, title and text become one row. |

File, Link, REST API and Web page have **Use test data**, which fills the form with a public example so you can try
a source without your own credentials.

**How unifying works.** One source is the *standard*: its format (columns, types, value scales) becomes the
project's baseline. Every other source is mapped onto it once. Columns whose names match apart from case and
punctuation are mapped automatically; the AI proposes the rest (renames, unit changes such as cents to dollars,
numbers stored as text, capitalisation) from statistics only, using the same safe operations as the repair agent.
A person reviews the steps and approves them. From then on every pull replays that recipe with no AI, checks the
result, and **Unify** combines all sources into one table (with a `_source` column) you can download. If a source
changes shape so a step no longer applies, the run says so instead of guessing.

**Sending unified data to your own database.** Under *Send unified data to* on the Sources tab, add a destination:
a connection URL (PostgreSQL/Supabase, MySQL, SQL Server, MongoDB, or any SQLAlchemy database), one table name, and
whether each unify **replaces** the table's contents or **adds** its rows. Every Unify then writes the result
there automatically (SQL writes are one transaction: all rows or none), and the report says what was sent. Data that
fails its checks is **held back** and never written. Only the named table is ever touched, and it is created on
the first send. The database user needs permission to write it.

**Safety.** Connection URLs and passwords stay in the server's database and are never sent back to the browser or
shown in error messages. Queries are read-only: a table name becomes `SELECT * FROM table`, anything else must be
one `SELECT` query, and it runs in a transaction that is rolled back (marked read-only on PostgreSQL). Use a
read-only database user anyway. Links must be public `https://` addresses. SQLite files must sit in the folder
named by `DATAPULSE_SQLITE_DIR`. At most `DATAPULSE_MAX_SOURCE_ROWS` rows (default 200,000) are read per pull.

**Adding a source type** is one file in `src/datapulse/sources/`: a class with the `fields` its form needs, a
`test()` and a `fetch()` that returns a table, decorated with `@register`. It appears in the dashboard
automatically. See `link.py` for the smallest example and `base.py` for the contract. The drivers install with
`pip install -e ".[sources]"` (the Docker images include them).

## Recognizing projects in any language

DataPulse does not assume a Python project. `datapulse scan` and `datapulse check --discover` recognize the
ecosystems and source languages in a repository and find the code that reads your data file and references its
columns, so reports can say *which files* a data change may break, whatever language they are written in.

```bash
datapulse scan examples/data/orders.csv --root examples/polyglot           # what reads this dataset?
datapulse scan examples/data/orders.csv --root examples/polyglot -o consumers.yml   # reviewable consumers YAML

datapulse baseline examples/data/orders.csv -c examples/datapulse.yml
datapulse check examples/data/orders_drifted.csv -c examples/datapulse.yml \
  --discover --scan-root examples/polyglot
```

| Recognized | How |
|---|---|
| **Languages** (by file extension) | Python, Jupyter notebooks, JavaScript, TypeScript, Java, Kotlin, Scala, Go, Rust, Ruby, R, C#, PHP, SQL, Julia, Swift |
| **Ecosystems** (by manifest file) | Node.js, TypeScript, Go modules, Cargo, Maven, Gradle, sbt, Bundler, Composer, pyproject/requirements, Julia, SwiftPM, R, .NET, dbt |

How it finds things (plain text search, no parsing):
1. A file is a **reader** if it mentions the data file's name or path (or, for SQL, the table name = file stem).
   Both the file being checked and the file the baseline was built from are searched for.
2. In readers, a column is **referenced** when it appears as a quoted string (`"status"`), an attribute
   (`row.status`, `df$status`, `:status` in Ruby), a bare word in SQL, or a camelCase/PascalCase spelling
   (`customer_id` -> `customerId`, `CustomerId`, `getCustomerId()`) in Java, Go, C#, JS and similar.
3. Excluded by default: dot-directories, `node_modules`, `venv`, `target`, `build`, `dist`, `vendor`, `obj`.
   Add more with `--exclude` or `discovery.exclude`. `--scope all` also reports non-reader files that match.

**These are leads, not facts.** The reports label them "detected by text search; heuristic, not verified". It
cannot follow data passed between modules, and misses dynamically built column names (`row[name]`). Generic
names (`id`, `status`) can match unrelated code. `scan -o` writes suggested `consumers:` to a **new** file and
never edits your `datapulse.yml`. Configuration lives in the `discovery:` section of `datapulse.yml`.

## Web dashboard and REST API

The same checks also run as a small self-hosted service: a React dashboard plus a FastAPI server that
stores projects, baselines and run history in SQLite. A project's rules are a normal `datapulse.yml`,
validated exactly like the CLI's, and each run returns the same JSON report as `datapulse check --json`.

```bash
docker compose up --build        # dashboard: http://localhost:8080   API docs: http://localhost:8000/docs
```

Without Docker (Python 3.10+, Node.js 20+):

```bash
pip install -e ".[web]"
uvicorn server.app:app --reload --port 8000
cd web && npm install && npm run dev    # http://localhost:5173 (proxies /api to port 8000)
```

In the dashboard: create a project (or **Try it with sample data**), drop a known-good file to save the baseline, then drop each new version to check it; the check starts as soon as the file lands. Each project has four tabs: **Checks** (results and history), **Baseline** (column profile, replace it), **Rules** (the project's `datapulse.yml`, plus `datapulse suggest` output to copy from; nothing is applied) and **Send data** (the ingest API). Uploads accept CSV, JSON (an array of row objects), JSONL/NDJSON and Parquet, up to 25 MB.

**Sending data from any language.** Creating a project returns an API key once (only its hash is stored).
Any service that can make an HTTP request can send rows:

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/projects/PROJECT_ID/ingest \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H 'X-DataPulse-Key: PROJECT_API_KEY' \
  -d '{"dataset_name": "orders", "role": "candidate",
       "rows": [{"order_id": 1, "customer_id": "C0001", "amount": 22.5, "status": "pending"}]}'
```

Send `"role": "baseline"` first (each re-baseline bumps `baseline_version`), then `candidate` batches; each
request takes up to 50,000 rows. The response is the JSON report (`status`, `issues`, `affected_consumers`, ...).
`"use_ai": true` adds AI explanations when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for the server (see `.env.example`).

Data is stored under `DATAPULSE_HOME` (default `.datapulse/`): uploaded files, baselines, reports and accounts.

### Running it for your team

Run **one** shared copy and have everyone open it, rather than each person running their own. Everyone then sees
the same projects, checks and AI triage, and only that one server needs an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

1. On a machine everyone can reach (a server, a cloud VM, or one teammate's computer on the office network), put
   the key in `.env` and run `docker compose up -d --build`.
2. Open `http://<that-machine>:8080`. The first person to open a fresh install creates the **admin** account.
3. The admin adds teammates under **Account & team** (name, email, temporary password). Teammates sign in and can
   change their password there.
4. On a failed check, anyone can set **Open / Acknowledged / Resolved**; the dashboard shows who did it and when,
   who ran each check, and who requested each AI triage.

### Deploying to the cloud (one permanent link for everyone)

`Dockerfile.app` packages the dashboard and the API into one container on one port, with all data (accounts,
projects, checks) on a volume at `/data`. `railway.json` configures [Railway](https://railway.com) to use it:

1. In Railway: **New Project → Deploy from GitHub repo** → pick this repository (and the branch to deploy).
2. On the service, open **Variables** and add `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (your key) and `DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES=1`.
   Add `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` only if your key isn't tied to a workspace.
3. Right-click the service → **Attach volume**, mount path `/data`. Without it, data is lost on every redeploy.
4. **Settings → Networking → Generate Domain**. Open that `https://...up.railway.app` link; the first person to open
   it creates the admin account, then adds teammates under **Account & team**.

Every push to the deployed branch redeploys automatically. Any other host that runs a Dockerfile and offers a
persistent volume works the same way (Fly.io, Render with a disk, a VM with `docker run -p 80:8000 -v dp:/data`).

Every `/api` route needs a signed-in user except `/api/health`, sign-in itself, and `/ingest`, which uses the
project's API key so other services can send data. Passwords are stored as scrypt hashes; sessions are HttpOnly
cookies that last 14 days. If the dashboard is reachable from the internet, serve it over HTTPS (for example behind
a reverse proxy) and set `DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES=1`.

## Running it without Python

DataPulse itself needs no Python in your project: it reads CSV/Parquet and YAML and adds nothing to your
dependency tree. Choose how to run it:

| You use... | Run DataPulse with | Needs on your machine |
|---|---|---|
| **GitHub Actions, no Python at all** | `uses: your-org/datapulse@v1` with `binary-url:` (see "Standalone executable") | nothing; a prebuilt executable is downloaded |
| **GitHub Actions** (any language) | `uses: your-org/datapulse@v1` (see below) | nothing; the action installs itself into an isolated virtualenv |
| **Any machine or CI, no Python** | download `datapulse-<os>-<arch>` from Releases | nothing |
| **Any CI or laptop** | Docker image: `docker run --rm -v "$PWD":/work datapulse check data.csv` | Docker |
| **Developers who just want the CLI** | `pipx install .` or `uvx --from . datapulse ...` | pipx or uv |
| **A Python project** | `pip install -e ".[dev]"` | Python 3.10+ |
| **A team that wants a dashboard / REST API** | `docker compose up --build` (see "Web dashboard and REST API") | Docker |

Scripts in any language can read results from the exit code (`0` / `1` / `2`) or from the JSON report
(`datapulse check data.csv --json report.json`; fields: `status`, `exit_code`, `counts`, `rows`, and per-issue
`check`, `severity`, `columns`, `measured`, `suggested_explanation`, `consumers`, `code_references`; plus top-level `config`, `schema_changed`, `affected_consumers`). The schema is versioned
(`schema_version: 1`).

Examples for other stacks are in `examples/workflows/`: `node.yml`, `go.yml` (monorepo with `working-directory`),
`java.yml`, and `docker.yml` (non-GitHub CI).

## Standalone executable (no Python needed)

DataPulse is built as a single executable with its own Python runtime bundled (about 180 MB, because it includes
pandas, Parquet support and scikit-learn). Teams in Go, Node, Java, Rust, .NET, etc. can run it without installing Python.

```bash
# use a release build
chmod +x datapulse-linux-x64 && ./datapulse-linux-x64 check data/orders.csv

# or build it yourself on your OS (inside a virtualenv)
python -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
bash scripts/build_binary.sh            # -> dist/datapulse (dist/datapulse.exe on Windows)
```

`.github/workflows/release.yml` builds Linux, macOS (arm64 and x64) and Windows executables with SHA-256
checksums and attaches them to each GitHub Release. Use them in Actions without Python:

```yaml
- uses: your-org/datapulse@v1
  with:
    data-file: data/orders.csv
    binary-url: https://github.com/your-org/datapulse/releases/download/v0.1.0/datapulse-linux-x64
    binary-sha256: <contents of datapulse-linux-x64.sha256>   # the step fails on a mismatch
```

Notes: the executable still needs `git` on `PATH` for `--baseline-ref` (GitHub runners have it). Builds are
per-OS and per-architecture, and a Linux build links against the glibc of the machine that built it, so the
release workflow builds on standard runners.

## Install

From a source checkout (Python 3.10+; other options in the table above):

```bash
git clone <this-repo> datapulse && cd datapulse
python -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate      # Windows: .venv\Scripts\activate
pip install -e ".[dev]"
datapulse --version
```

In another project, any language: `pipx install /path/to/datapulse` (or `pipx install git+https://github.com/<you>/datapulse`).
Docker: `docker build -t datapulse .` then `docker run --rm --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -v "$PWD":/work datapulse check data/orders.csv`.

## Quick start (sample data included)

```bash
# 1. Create a versioned baseline from a known-good file
datapulse baseline examples/data/orders.csv --config examples/datapulse.yml

# 2. Check the same file: passes, exit code 0
datapulse check examples/data/orders.csv --config examples/datapulse.yml

# 3. Check a drifted file: reports issues, exit code 1
datapulse check examples/data/orders_drifted.csv --config examples/datapulse.yml
echo $?    # 1

# 4. Get reviewable rule suggestions (never edits your config)
datapulse suggest examples/data/orders.csv --output suggested.yml

# 5. Run the full test suite
datapulse test
```

## Using it in your own project

```bash
datapulse init                          # writes a commented datapulse.yml (never overwrites without --force)
datapulse baseline data/orders.csv      # writes .datapulse/baseline.json; commit it
datapulse check data/orders.csv         # terminal report + datapulse-report.md
```

Re-running `datapulse baseline` after an intentional data change bumps `baseline_version` and replaces the file.

## Commands

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `datapulse init` | Write a starter `datapulse.yml`. |
| `datapulse baseline <file> [-c CONFIG] [-o PATH]` | Profile a known-good file into a versioned baseline JSON. |
| `datapulse check <file> [-c CONFIG] [--baseline PATH] [--baseline-ref REF] [--config-ref REF] [--markdown PATH] [--json PATH] [--discover] [--scan-root DIR] [--reference FILE] [--triage] [--ai]` | Run all checks. `--reference` (the known-good data file) adds the ML drift check; `--triage` runs the AI agent on failures. |
| `datapulse scan <file> [--root DIR] [--scope readers\|all] [--exclude DIR] [-o FILE]` | Recognize languages in a repo and find code that reads the data file. |
| `datapulse fix <file> -o FILE [--reference FILE]` | AI repair agent: writes a repaired copy to a **new** file and re-checks it. |
| `datapulse draft-rules "<request>" [--data FILE] [-o FILE]` | Plain-English request -> complete, validated `datapulse.yml` (AI). Prints it or writes a **new** file. |
| `datapulse suggest <file> [-o FILE] [--force] [--ai]` | Propose rules from the profile. Prints YAML or writes a **new** file; refuses to touch your config. |
| `datapulse github-outputs <report.json>` | Print `status`, `issue-count`, `schema-changed`, `affected-consumers` as `key=value` lines for `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` (values are sanitized). |
| `datapulse comment <report.md> [--repo OWNER/NAME] [--pr N]` | Create/update the PR comment (uses `GITHUB_TOKEN`). |
| `datapulse test` | Run the DataPulse test suite (source checkout with `.[dev]`). |

**Exit codes:** `0` passed · `1` issues at or above `fail_on` · `2` bad file, config, baseline, or environment.

## Checks

| Check (`datapulse.yml` key) | Detects | Key settings |
|---|---|---|
| `schema` | Missing columns, added columns, type changes | severity per kind |
| `row_count` | Row count change vs. baseline, minimum rows | `max_change_pct`, `min_rows` |
| `null_rate` | Null-rate increase (absolute) | `max_increase`, per-column `columns` |
| `categorical` | Values not seen in the baseline (low-cardinality text columns) | `max_unexpected_rate`, `columns` |
| `duplicates` | Duplicate key values or whole rows | `columns`, `max_duplicate_rate` |
| `distribution` | Numeric shift: Population Stability Index (PSI) and mean shift in baseline std units | `psi_threshold`, `max_mean_shift_std`, `columns` |
| `ml_drift` | Learned drift: a model separates baseline rows from new rows (needs the baseline's rows) | `max_auc`, `max_rows`, `exclude` |

Severities are `critical`, `warning`, `info`, or `off`. `fail_on` (`critical` by default; also `warning`, `info`,
`never`) decides which severity produces exit code 1.

Behaviour notes:
- A whole-number float column (what pandas produces when an integer column gains nulls) counts as `integer`, so a
  few nulls do not look like a type change. Float to integer narrowing is not reported.
- Categorical values are only tracked for text columns with at most `max_categories` (default 50) distinct values.
- Duplicates by key columns are absolute; whole-row duplicates are compared with the baseline's duplicate rate.
- Sequential identifier columns (all-unique integers) are skipped by `distribution` unless named in `columns`.

See `examples/datapulse.yml` for a complete configuration.

## Downstream consumers

```yaml
consumers:
  revenue_dashboard:
    type: dashboard
    owner: finance-analytics
    columns: [amount, created_at]
```

When a column fails, every consumer listing it is shown. Dataset-wide issues (row count, whole-row duplicates)
list all consumers. Reports separate **Measured from the data** (facts) from **Possible explanation (suggested,
not verified)**.

## GitHub Action

`action.yml` is a composite action. On pull requests it:

1. fetches the target branch and reads **both the baseline and `datapulse.yml` from `origin/<base>`**, so a PR cannot pass by re-baselining its own data or by loosening its own thresholds (rule changes take effect once merged; set `config-from-base: "false"` to opt out),
2. runs `datapulse check` on the changed file,
3. posts or updates one PR comment (identified by a hidden marker) and writes the report to the job summary,
4. fails the job if issues reach `fail_on`.

Setup:

1. Commit `datapulse.yml` and `.datapulse/baseline.json` to your default branch.
2. Copy `examples/workflows/datapulse.yml` to `.github/workflows/datapulse.yml` and set `uses:` to the repository
   hosting this action.

```yaml
permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
steps:
  - uses: actions/checkout@v4
  - uses: your-org/datapulse@v1
    with:
      data-file: data/orders.csv
```

Inputs: `data-file` (required), `config`, `baseline`, `baseline-ref`, `config-from-base`, `working-directory`, `discover`, `setup-python`,
`binary-url`, `binary-sha256`, `python-version`, `comment`, `ai`, `github-token`. Outputs: `status` (`passed`/`failed`/`error`), `issue-count`, `schema-changed` (`true`/`false`), `affected-consumers` (comma-separated),
`exit-code`, `report-markdown`, `report-json`. Use them to gate later steps:

```yaml
- id: dq
  uses: your-org/datapulse@v1
  with: { data-file: data/orders.csv }
- if: always() && steps.dq.outputs.schema-changed == 'true'
  run: echo "Schema changed; notify: ${{ steps.dq.outputs.affected-consumers }}"
```

The PR comment and report start with a **Pull request impact** section (schema changes and affected consumers).
If a ref's config is missing, the check says so and uses the working-tree config; if the ref itself was not
fetched it fails with exit code 2 rather than silently trusting the PR.

The action is language-agnostic: it installs DataPulse into its own virtualenv under `$RUNNER_TEMP` (it never
touches your project's dependencies or lockfiles), writes its reports outside your working tree, and accepts
`working-directory` for monorepos. Set `setup-python: "false"` to use the runner's own `python3` instead of
`actions/setup-python`, which otherwise puts a Python on `PATH` for the rest of the job.
The default `github.token` is used; no tokens are stored. On fork PRs the token is read-only, so the comment step
is skipped without failing the job and the report remains in the job summary.

## AI explanations from the CLI

```bash
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...        # name configurable via ai.api_key_env
datapulse check data.csv --ai       # or ai.enabled: true in datapulse.yml
datapulse suggest data.csv --ai
```

- Sent: column names, aggregate statistics, and numeric metrics of detected issues.
- Never sent: raw rows, category values, or example values (a test enforces this).
- AI text is labelled "AI-generated, unverified". With no key, or if the call fails, the deterministic explanations are used.
- `DATAPULSE_AI_URL` overrides the endpoint (for a proxy). `report.show_values: false` also hides example values from reports.

Datasets are never uploaded anywhere by DataPulse. Baselines contain aggregates plus the category labels of
low-cardinality text columns, so avoid baselining files where such labels are sensitive.

## Development

```bash
pip install -e ".[dev,web]"   # web is only needed for tests/test_server.py
datapulse test          # or: pytest -q
cd web && npm install && npm run build   # type-check and build the dashboard
python examples/generate_samples.py   # regenerate sample data
```

# Sample DataPulse configuration for examples/data/orders.csv
version: 1
baseline: .datapulse/baseline.json
fail_on: critical

checks:
  schema:
    missing_columns: critical
    added_columns: warning
    type_changes: critical
  row_count:
    severity: warning
    max_change_pct: 15
  null_rate:
    severity: critical
    max_increase: 0.02
    columns:
      customer_id: 0.0
  categorical:
    severity: warning
    max_unexpected_rate: 0.0
    columns: [status, country]
  duplicates:
    severity: critical
    columns: [order_id]
  distribution:
    severity: warning
    psi_threshold: 0.2
    max_mean_shift_std: 1.0

consumers:
  revenue_dashboard:
    type: dashboard
    owner: finance-analytics
    description: Daily revenue by country
    columns: [amount, created_at, country, status]
  churn_model:
    type: model
    owner: data-science
    columns: [customer_id, amount, quantity, created_at]
  fulfillment_service:
    type: service
    owner: logistics
    columns: [order_id, status, quantity]

report:
  markdown: datapulse-report.md
  max_examples: 5
  show_values: true

ai:
  enabled: false

"""Regenerate the sample datasets: python examples/generate_samples.py"""
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

OUT = Path(__file__).parent / "data"


def build(n: int = 500, seed: int = 42) -> pd.DataFrame:
    rs = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    return pd.DataFrame({
        "order_id": np.arange(1000, 1000 + n),
        "customer_id": [f"C{i:04d}" for i in rs.randint(0, 120, n)],
        "status": rs.choice(["pending", "shipped", "delivered", "cancelled"], n, p=[0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.1]),
        "amount": np.round(rs.lognormal(4, 0.5, n), 2),
        "quantity": rs.randint(1, 6, n),
        "country": rs.choice(["US", "CA", "GB", "DE"], n),
        "created_at": pd.date_range("2026-09-01", periods=n, freq="30min").strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"),
    })


def drift(df: pd.DataFrame, seed: int = 7) -> pd.DataFrame:
    rs = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    d = df.iloc[:380].copy()
    d = d.drop(columns=["country"])                                  # missing column
    d["coupon_code"] = rs.choice(["SAVE10", "WELCOME"], len(d))      # added column
    d["quantity"] = d["quantity"].astype(str) + " units"             # type change
    d.loc[d.sample(frac=0.35, random_state=1).index, "customer_id"] = np.nan  # null spike
    d["amount"] = (d["amount"] * 100).round(0)                       # unit change (dollars -> cents)
    d.loc[d.sample(frac=0.08, random_state=2).index, "status"] = "refunded_pending"  # new category
    dup = d.sample(10, random_state=3).index
    d.loc[dup, "order_id"] = d["order_id"].iloc[0]                   # duplicate keys
    return d


if __name__ == "__main__":
    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    base = build()
    base.to_csv(OUT / "orders.csv", index=False)
    drift(base).to_csv(OUT / "orders_drifted.csv", index=False)
    print("wrote", OUT / "orders.csv", OUT / "orders_drifted.csv")

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Build a standalone executable (Python runtime bundled): dist/datapulse[.exe]
# Usage: scripts/build_binary.sh      (run from the repository root)
set -euo pipefail
python -m pip install --quiet ".[build]"
python -m PyInstaller --noconfirm --clean --onefile --name datapulse \
  --paths src \
  --collect-all pyarrow \
  --collect-submodules pandas \
  --collect-submodules sklearn --collect-data sklearn \
  --collect-submodules anthropic \
  --exclude-module tkinter --exclude-module matplotlib --exclude-module IPython --exclude-module pytest \
  packaging/entry.py
echo "Built: $(ls dist/datapulse*)"

FILE [18/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\server\app.py

"""DataPulse web API: projects, baselines, checks, run history and JSON ingestion from any language.

It uses the same engine as the CLI (`datapulse baseline` / `datapulse check`), so a project's
`datapulse.yml`, its baseline and its reports mean exactly the same thing in the dashboard, the CLI
and the GitHub Action. Data is stored under $DATAPULSE_HOME (default `.datapulse/`) in SQLite.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import os
import secrets
import shutil
import sqlite3
import uuid
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Literal, Optional

import pandas as pd
from fastapi import FastAPI, File, Form, Header, HTTPException, Request, UploadFile
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse, JSONResponse
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from datapulse import __version__
from datapulse.agent import TriageContext, run_triage
from datapulse.ai import draft_rules, get_api_key
from datapulse.config import STARTER_CONFIG, Config
from datapulse.errors import DataPulseError
from datapulse.fix import RepairSession, run_repair
from datapulse.profile import profile_dataframe
from datapulse.suggest import render_yaml, suggest_rules
from datapulse.unify import SOURCE_COLUMN

from .auth import current_user, needs_login, router as auth_router
from .core import (MAX_INGEST_ROWS, MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES, ROOT, ai_call, baseline_summary, dataset_rows, handle_data,
                   loads, project_config, project_row, record_dataset, run_check, save_upload,
                   load_upload)
from .db import configure_sources, connect, init_db, now_iso, samples_dir, upload_dir
from .destinations import router as destinations_router
from .sources import add_sample_sources, remove_uploaded_files, router as sources_router


@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(_: FastAPI):
    init_db()
    configure_sources()
    yield


app = FastAPI(title="DataPulse API", version=__version__, lifespan=lifespan,
              description="Data-quality checks for CSV, JSON and Parquet data from any language.")
app.add_middleware(
    CORSMiddleware,
    allow_origins=os.getenv("DATAPULSE_CORS_ORIGINS", "http://localhost:5173,http://127.0.0.1:5173").split(","),
    allow_methods=["*"],
    allow_headers=["*"],
    allow_credentials=True,
)
app.include_router(auth_router)
app.include_router(sources_router)
app.include_router(destinations_router)


@app.middleware("http")
async def require_login(request: Request, call_next):
    if needs_login(request.url.path, request.method):
        request.state.user = current_user(request)
        if request.state.user is None:
            return JSONResponse({"detail": "Please sign in"}, status_code=401)
    return await call_next(request)


class CreateProject(BaseModel):
    name: str = Field(min_length=2, max_length=80)


class SaveConfig(BaseModel):
    config_yaml: str = Field(max_length=20_000)


class DraftRules(BaseModel):
    request: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=2_000)
    config_yaml: str = Field(max_length=20_000)  # the editor's current text, saved or not


class Review(BaseModel):
    status: Literal["open", "acknowledged", "resolved"]


class IngestRows(BaseModel):
    dataset_name: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)
    role: Literal["baseline", "candidate"] = "candidate"
    rows: list[dict] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=MAX_INGEST_ROWS)
    use_ai: bool = False


@app.get("/api/health")
def health() -> dict:
    return {"status": "ok", "version": __version__, "ai": bool(get_api_key(Config()))}


@app.get("/api/projects")
def list_projects() -> list[dict]:
    with connect() as db:
        rows = db.execute(
            "SELECT p.id, p.name, p.created_at, p.baseline_json IS NOT NULL AS has_baseline, "
            "(SELECT COUNT(*) FROM runs r WHERE r.project_id = p.id) AS run_count, "
            "(SELECT status FROM runs r WHERE r.project_id = p.id ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC LIMIT 1) AS latest_status "
            "FROM projects p ORDER BY p.created_at DESC, p.rowid DESC"
        ).fetchall()
    return [{"id": r["id"], "name": r["name"], "created_at": r["created_at"], "has_baseline": bool(r["has_baseline"]),
             "run_count": r["run_count"], "latest_status": r["latest_status"]} for r in rows]


def _create_project(name: str, config_yaml: str = STARTER_CONFIG) -> dict:
    project_id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]
    api_key = "dp_" + secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
    created = now_iso()
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("INSERT INTO projects (id, name, api_key_hash, config_yaml, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
                   (project_id, name, hashlib.sha256(api_key.encode()).hexdigest(),
                    config_yaml, created))
    # The key is returned once; only its hash is stored.
    return {"id": project_id, "name": name, "api_key": api_key, "created_at": created}


@app.post("/api/projects")
def create_project(payload: CreateProject) -> dict:
    name = payload.name.strip()
    if len(name) < 2:
        raise HTTPException(422, "Project name must contain at least two non-space characters")
    return _create_project(name)


@app.get("/api/projects/{project_id}")
def get_project(project_id: str) -> dict:
    project = project_row(project_id)
    with connect() as db:
        datasets = db.execute("SELECT id, name, role, row_count, columns_json, created_at FROM datasets "
                              "WHERE project_id = ? ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC", (project_id,)).fetchall()
        runs = db.execute("SELECT id, status, issue_count, created_at, report_json, created_by, review_status, "
                          "review_by, review_at FROM runs "
                          "WHERE project_id = ? ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC LIMIT 30", (project_id,)).fetchall()
    baseline = loads(project["baseline_json"])
    cfg = project_config(project["config_yaml"])
    return {
        "id": project["id"], "name": project["name"], "created_at": project["created_at"],
        "has_baseline": baseline is not None,
        "baseline": baseline_summary(baseline) if baseline else None,
        "config_yaml": project["config_yaml"],
        "fail_on": cfg.fail_on,
        "consumers": [{"name": c.name, "type": c.type, "owner": c.owner, "columns": c.columns} for c in cfg.consumers],
        "datasets": [{"id": d["id"], "name": d["name"], "role": d["role"], "row_count": d["row_count"],
                      "columns": json.loads(d["columns_json"]), "created_at": d["created_at"]} for d in datasets],
        "runs": [{"id": r["id"], "status": r["status"], "issue_count": r["issue_count"], "created_at": r["created_at"],
                  "created_by": r["created_by"], "review": {"status": r["review_status"], "by": r["review_by"],
                                                            "at": r["review_at"]},
                  "report": json.loads(r["report_json"])} for r in runs],
    }

async def _upload(request: Request, project_id: str, file: UploadFile, name: Optional[str], role: str,
                  use_ai: bool = False) -> dict:
    project_row(project_id)
    path = save_upload(file.filename or "", await file.read(MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES + 1))
    return handle_data(project_id, name or file.filename or role, path, role, request.state.user["name"], use_ai)

@app.delete("/api/projects/{project_id}")
def delete_project(project_id: str) -> dict:
    """Delete a project with its datasets, runs and uploaded files (files outside the upload folder are kept)."""
    project_row(project_id)
    uploads = upload_dir()
    with connect() as db:
        files = [Path(r["filename"]) for r in db.execute("SELECT filename FROM datasets WHERE project_id = ?",
                                                          (project_id,))]
        remove_uploaded_files(db.execute("SELECT kind, settings_json FROM sources WHERE project_id = ?",
                                         (project_id,)).fetchall())
        for table, column in (("runs", "project_id"), ("sources", "project_id"), ("destinations", "project_id"),
                              ("datasets", "project_id"),
                              ("projects", "id")):
            db.execute(f"DELETE FROM {table} WHERE {column} = ?", (project_id,))
    for f in files:
        if f.parent == uploads:
            f.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    shutil.rmtree(samples_dir() / project_id, ignore_errors=True)
    return {"deleted": True}


@app.post("/api/projects/{project_id}/ingest")
def ingest_rows(project_id: str, payload: IngestRows, x_datapulse_key: Optional[str] = Header(None)) -> dict:
    """Send rows as JSON from any language. Requires the project's X-DataPulse-Key."""
    project = project_row(project_id)
    if not x_datapulse_key or not secrets.compare_digest(
            hashlib.sha256(x_datapulse_key.encode()).hexdigest(), project["api_key_hash"]):
        raise HTTPException(401, "A valid X-DataPulse-Key header is required")
    body = "\n".join(json.dumps(row, ensure_ascii=False) for row in payload.rows)
    if len(body.encode("utf-8")) > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES:
        raise HTTPException(413, "JSON batch exceeds the 25 MB limit")
    path = upload_dir() / f"{uuid.uuid4().hex}.jsonl"
    path.write_text(body + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    return handle_data(project_id, payload.dataset_name, path, payload.role, "API", payload.use_ai)


@app.post("/api/demo/seed")
def seed_demo(request: Request) -> dict:
    """A sample project: three databases holding the same orders in different formats (the first is the
    standard), with the example datapulse.yml."""
    config_text = (ROOT / "examples" / "datapulse.yml").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    project = _create_project("Orders (sample)", config_text)
    add_sample_sources(project["id"], request.state.user["name"])
    return {**project, "has_baseline": True}


def _run_row(project_id: str, run_id: str) -> sqlite3.Row:
    with connect() as db:
        run = db.execute("SELECT * FROM runs WHERE id = ? AND project_id = ?", (run_id, project_id)).fetchone()
    if run is None:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Run not found")
    return run


def _failed_run(project_id: str, run_id: str) -> tuple:
    run = _run_row(project_id, run_id)
    doc = json.loads(run["report_json"])
    if not doc["issues"]:
        raise HTTPException(409, "This run passed cleanly; there is nothing to investigate")
    return run, doc


def _run_data(run: sqlite3.Row) -> Optional[pd.DataFrame]:
    """The checked data of a run, without the _source tag a unified table carries."""
    df = dataset_rows(run["dataset_id"])
    return None if df is None else df.drop(columns=[SOURCE_COLUMN], errors="ignore")


def _triage(project: sqlite3.Row, run: sqlite3.Row, doc: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """The triage agent's incident note for a failed run."""
    with connect() as db:
        history = db.execute("SELECT created_at, status, issue_count, report_json FROM runs WHERE project_id = ? "
                             "AND id != ? ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC LIMIT 15",
                             (project["id"], run["id"])).fetchall()
    cfg = project_config(project["config_yaml"])
    current = _run_data(run)
    ctx = TriageContext(
        doc, json.loads(project["baseline_json"])["profile"],
        profile_dataframe(current, cfg.max_categories) if current is not None else None,
        history=[{"when": h["created_at"], "status": h["status"], "issue_count": h["issue_count"],
                  "failing": sorted({f"{i['check']}:{','.join(i['columns'])}" for i in json.loads(h["report_json"])["issues"]})}
                 for h in history],
        consumers=[vars(c) for c in cfg.consumers])
    return run_triage(ctx, cfg)


def _repair(project: sqlite3.Row, run: sqlite3.Row, doc: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """The repair agent's fixed copy of a failed run, saved as a dataset. The original is never changed."""
    df = _run_data(run)
    if df is None:
        raise DataPulseError("The file for this check is no longer stored, so it can't be repaired")
    session = RepairSession(df, json.loads(project["baseline_json"]), project_config(project["config_yaml"]),
                            reference=dataset_rows(project["baseline_dataset_id"]))
    result = run_repair(session)
    fixed = result.pop("fixed_frame")
    stem = Path(doc.get("data_file") or "data").stem
    path = upload_dir() / f"{uuid.uuid4().hex}.csv"
    fixed.to_csv(path, index=False)
    dataset_id = record_dataset(project["id"], f"{stem} (fixed).csv", path, "fixed", fixed)
    return {**result, "dataset_id": dataset_id, "file_name": f"{stem}-fixed.csv"}


AGENTS = {"triage": _triage, "fix": _repair}


def _save_report(run_id: str, doc: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("UPDATE runs SET report_json = ? WHERE id = ?", (json.dumps(doc), run_id))


def _one_agent(project_id: str, run_id: str, request: Request, name: str) -> dict:
    project = project_row(project_id)
    run, doc = _failed_run(project_id, run_id)
    doc[name] = {**ai_call(lambda: AGENTS[name](project, run, doc)), "by": request.state.user["name"], "at": now_iso()}
    _save_report(run_id, doc)
    return doc[name]


@app.post("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/investigate")
def investigate_run(project_id: str, run_id: str, request: Request) -> dict:
    """Investigate with AI: the triage agent diagnoses the run while the repair agent fixes a copy, in parallel.

    Parts already done are kept. If one agent fails the other's result is still saved and the error is returned.
    """
    project = project_row(project_id)
    run, doc = _failed_run(project_id, run_id)
    todo = [name for name in AGENTS if not doc.get(name)]
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
        futures = {name: pool.submit(AGENTS[name], project, run, doc) for name in todo}
    errors = {}
    for name, future in futures.items():
        try:
            doc[name] = {**future.result(), "by": request.state.user["name"], "at": now_iso()}
        except DataPulseError as exc:
            errors[name] = str(exc)
    if todo and len(errors) == len(todo):
        detail = "; ".join(dict.fromkeys(errors.values()))
        raise HTTPException(503 if "environment variable" in detail else 502, detail)
    _save_report(run_id, doc)
    return {"triage": doc.get("triage"), "fix": doc.get("fix"), "errors": errors}


@app.post("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/triage")
def triage_run(project_id: str, run_id: str, request: Request) -> dict:
    """Only the triage agent's incident note (the dashboard uses /investigate)."""
    return _one_agent(project_id, run_id, request, "triage")


@app.post("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/fix")
def fix_run(project_id: str, run_id: str, request: Request) -> dict:
    """Only the repair agent's fixed copy (the dashboard uses /investigate)."""
    return _one_agent(project_id, run_id, request, "fix")


def _fixed_file(project_id: str, run_id: str) -> tuple:
    """The repaired file of a run: (path, download name)."""
    fix = json.loads(_run_row(project_id, run_id)["report_json"]).get("fix")
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT filename FROM datasets WHERE id = ?", (fix["dataset_id"],)).fetchone() if fix else None
    if row is None or not Path(row["filename"]).exists():
        raise HTTPException(404, "This check has no repaired file yet")
    return Path(row["filename"]), fix["file_name"]


@app.get("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/data")
def download_run_data(project_id: str, run_id: str) -> FileResponse:
    """The data a run checked, e.g. a unified table or a source pulled from a database."""
    run = _run_row(project_id, run_id)
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT name, filename FROM datasets WHERE id = ?", (run["dataset_id"],)).fetchone()
    if row is None or not Path(row["filename"]).exists():
        raise HTTPException(404, "The data for this check is no longer stored")
    path = Path(row["filename"])
    return FileResponse(path, filename=f"{Path(row['name']).stem}{path.suffix}")


@app.get("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/fixed")
def download_fixed(project_id: str, run_id: str) -> FileResponse:
    path, name = _fixed_file(project_id, run_id)
    return FileResponse(path, media_type="text/csv", filename=name)


@app.post("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/fixed/check")
def check_fixed(project_id: str, run_id: str, request: Request) -> dict:
    """Run a normal check on the repaired file, so it shows up in the history next to the original."""
    path, name = _fixed_file(project_id, run_id)
    return run_check(project_row(project_id), name, path, load_upload(path), False, request.state.user["name"])


@app.put("/api/projects/{project_id}/runs/{run_id}/review")
def review_run(project_id: str, run_id: str, payload: Review, request: Request) -> dict:
    """Shared issue status, so the team can see who is handling a failed check."""
    review = {"status": payload.status, "by": request.state.user["name"], "at": now_iso()}
    with connect() as db:
        updated = db.execute("UPDATE runs SET review_status = ?, review_by = ?, review_at = ? "
                             "WHERE id = ? AND project_id = ?",
                             (review["status"], review["by"], review["at"], run_id, project_id)).rowcount
    if not updated:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Run not found")
    return review

FILE [19/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\server\auth.py

"""Dashboard accounts: email + password, cookie sessions.

The first account created on a fresh install is the admin; the admin adds teammates. Every /api route needs a
signed-in user except health, sign-in itself, and /ingest, which uses the project's API key instead.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import hmac
import os
import re
import secrets
import uuid
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from typing import Any, Dict, Literal, Optional

from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request, Response
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from .db import connect, now_iso

COOKIE = "dp_session"
SESSION_DAYS = 14
PUBLIC_PATHS = {"/api/health", "/api/auth/status", "/api/auth/setup", "/api/auth/login"}
INGEST_PATH = re.compile(r"^/api/projects/[\w-]+/ingest$")

router = APIRouter(prefix="/api")


class Credentials(BaseModel):
    email: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=200)
    password: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=200)


class NewUser(BaseModel):
    name: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=80)
    email: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=200)
    password: str = Field(min_length=8, max_length=200)
    role: Literal["admin", "member"] = "member"


class PasswordChange(BaseModel):
    current_password: str = Field(max_length=200)
    new_password: str = Field(min_length=8, max_length=200)


def hash_password(password: str) -> str:
    salt = secrets.token_bytes(16)
    digest = hashlib.scrypt(password.encode(), salt=salt, n=2**14, r=8, p=1, dklen=32)
    return f"scrypt${salt.hex()}${digest.hex()}"


def verify_password(password: str, stored: str) -> bool:
    try:
        _, salt, digest = stored.split("$")
        actual = hashlib.scrypt(password.encode(), salt=bytes.fromhex(salt), n=2**14, r=8, p=1, dklen=32)
    except ValueError:
        return False
    return hmac.compare_digest(actual.hex(), digest)


_DUMMY_HASH = hash_password(secrets.token_hex(8))  # keeps unknown-email logins as slow as wrong-password ones


def _email(raw: str) -> str:
    email = raw.strip().lower()
    if not re.fullmatch(r"[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+", email):
        raise HTTPException(422, "Enter a valid email address")
    return email


def _public(row) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    return {"id": row["id"], "name": row["name"], "email": row["email"], "role": row["role"]}


def _token_hash(token: str) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(token.encode()).hexdigest()


def current_user(request: Request) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
    token = request.cookies.get(COOKIE)
    if not token:
        return None
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT u.* FROM sessions s JOIN users u ON u.id = s.user_id "
                         "WHERE s.token_hash = ? AND s.expires_at > ?", (_token_hash(token), now_iso())).fetchone()
    return _public(row) if row else None


def needs_login(path: str, method: str) -> bool:
    return (path.startswith("/api/") and method != "OPTIONS" and path not in PUBLIC_PATHS
            and not INGEST_PATH.match(path))


def _start_session(response: Response, user_id: str) -> None:
    token = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
    expires = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(days=SESSION_DAYS)
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("DELETE FROM sessions WHERE expires_at <= ?", (now_iso(),))
        db.execute("INSERT INTO sessions VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
                   (_token_hash(token), user_id, expires.replace(microsecond=0).isoformat()))
    # Secure cookies need HTTPS; turn them on with DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES=1 when serving over HTTPS.
    response.set_cookie(COOKIE, token, max_age=SESSION_DAYS * 86400, httponly=True, samesite="lax",
                        secure=os.getenv("DATAPULSE_SECURE_COOKIES") == "1")


def _create_user(name: str, email: str, password: str, role: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    user = {"id": uuid.uuid4().hex[:12], "name": name.strip(), "email": _email(email), "role": role}
    with connect() as db:
        if db.execute("SELECT 1 FROM users WHERE email = ?", (user["email"],)).fetchone():
            raise HTTPException(409, "Someone with that email already has an account")
        db.execute("INSERT INTO users VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
                   (user["id"], user["email"], user["name"], hash_password(password), role, now_iso()))
    return user


def _admin(request: Request) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    if request.state.user["role"] != "admin":
        raise HTTPException(403, "Only an admin can manage the team")
    return request.state.user


def _user_count() -> int:
    with connect() as db:
        return db.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users").fetchone()[0]


@router.get("/auth/status")
def auth_status(request: Request) -> dict:
    return {"setup_needed": _user_count() == 0, "user": current_user(request)}


@router.post("/auth/setup")
def setup(payload: NewUser, response: Response) -> dict:
    """Create the first (admin) account. Only works while no account exists."""
    if _user_count():
        raise HTTPException(409, "This DataPulse already has an admin; sign in instead")
    user = _create_user(payload.name, payload.email, payload.password, "admin")
    _start_session(response, user["id"])
    return user


@router.post("/auth/login")
def login(payload: Credentials, response: Response) -> dict:
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM users WHERE email = ?", (payload.email.strip().lower(),)).fetchone()
    if not verify_password(payload.password, row["password_hash"] if row else _DUMMY_HASH) or row is None:
        raise HTTPException(401, "Wrong email or password")
    _start_session(response, row["id"])
    return _public(row)


@router.post("/auth/logout")
def logout(request: Request, response: Response) -> dict:
    token = request.cookies.get(COOKIE)
    if token:
        with connect() as db:
            db.execute("DELETE FROM sessions WHERE token_hash = ?", (_token_hash(token),))
    response.delete_cookie(COOKIE)
    return {"signed_out": True}


@router.put("/auth/password")
def change_password(payload: PasswordChange, request: Request) -> dict:
    user_id = request.state.user["id"]
    with connect() as db:
        stored = db.execute("SELECT password_hash FROM users WHERE id = ?", (user_id,)).fetchone()["password_hash"]
        if not verify_password(payload.current_password, stored):
            raise HTTPException(403, "Your current password is not correct")
        db.execute("UPDATE users SET password_hash = ? WHERE id = ?", (hash_password(payload.new_password), user_id))
    return {"changed": True}


@router.get("/users")
def list_users(request: Request) -> list:
    _admin(request)
    with connect() as db:
        return [_public(r) for r in db.execute("SELECT * FROM users ORDER BY created_at")]


@router.post("/users")
def add_user(payload: NewUser, request: Request) -> dict:
    _admin(request)
    return _create_user(payload.name, payload.email, payload.password, payload.role)


@router.delete("/users/{user_id}")
def remove_user(user_id: str, request: Request) -> dict:
    if _admin(request)["id"] == user_id:
        raise HTTPException(409, "You can't remove your own account")
    with connect() as db:
        if db.execute("DELETE FROM users WHERE id = ?", (user_id,)).rowcount == 0:
            raise HTTPException(404, "User not found")
        db.execute("DELETE FROM sessions WHERE user_id = ?", (user_id,))
    return {"removed": True}

FILE [20/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\server\core.py

"""Shared project logic for the API routers: loading projects, saving datasets and baselines, running checks."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import uuid
from dataclasses import replace
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional

import pandas as pd
from fastapi import HTTPException

from datapulse.ai import explain_issues
from datapulse.baseline import build_baseline, describe
from datapulse.checks import run_checks
from datapulse.config import Config, parse_config_text
from datapulse.errors import DataPulseError
from datapulse.io import SUPPORTED, load_dataframe
from datapulse.profile import chart_data
from datapulse.report import render_json, render_markdown

from .db import connect, now_iso, upload_dir

MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024
MAX_INGEST_ROWS = 50_000
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
CONFIG_LABEL = "project config (dashboard)"


def loads(text: Optional[str]) -> Any:
    return json.loads(text) if text else None


def project_row(project_id: str) -> sqlite3.Row:
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM projects WHERE id = ?", (project_id,)).fetchone()
    if row is None:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Project not found")
    return row


def project_config(text: str) -> Config:
    try:
        return parse_config_text(text, CONFIG_LABEL)
    except DataPulseError as exc:
        raise HTTPException(422, str(exc)) from exc


def save_upload(filename: str, content: bytes) -> Path:
    suffix = Path(filename).suffix.lower()
    if suffix not in SUPPORTED:
        raise HTTPException(415, "Supported data files: CSV, JSON, JSONL/NDJSON, and Parquet")
    if len(content) > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES:
        raise HTTPException(413, "Upload exceeds the 25 MB limit")
    path = upload_dir() / f"{uuid.uuid4().hex}{suffix}"
    path.write_bytes(content)
    return path


def load_upload(path: Path) -> pd.DataFrame:
    try:
        return load_dataframe(path)
    except DataPulseError as exc:
        # Report the problem without exposing the server-side storage path.
        raise HTTPException(400, str(exc).replace(str(path), "the uploaded file")) from exc


def record_dataset(project_id: str, name: str, path: Path, role: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> str:
    dataset_id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("INSERT INTO datasets VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
                   (dataset_id, project_id, name, str(path), role, len(df), json.dumps(list(df.columns)), now_iso()))
    return dataset_id


def baseline_summary(data: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """What the dashboard shows about a baseline: aggregates only, no category values or histograms."""
    prof = data["profile"]
    return {
        "baseline_version": data.get("baseline_version", 1),
        "created_at": data.get("created_at"),
        "source": data.get("source", {}).get("file"),
        "rows": prof["rows"],
        "columns": {name: {k: info.get(k) for k in ("kind", "dtype", "null_rate", "unique_count")}
                    for name, info in prof["columns"].items()},
    }


def save_baseline(project: sqlite3.Row, name: str, path: Path, df: pd.DataFrame) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    if len(df) == 0:
        raise HTTPException(400, f"{name} has no rows; a baseline needs data.")
    cfg = project_config(project["config_yaml"])
    previous = loads(project["baseline_json"])
    version = previous["baseline_version"] + 1 if previous else 1
    data = build_baseline(df, path, version, cfg.max_categories)
    data["source"]["file"] = name  # the name the user knows, not the storage path
    dataset_id = record_dataset(project["id"], name, path, "baseline", df)
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("UPDATE projects SET baseline_json = ?, baseline_dataset_id = ? WHERE id = ?",
                   (json.dumps(data), dataset_id, project["id"]))
    return {"dataset_id": dataset_id, "role": "baseline", "baseline": baseline_summary(data)}


def dataset_rows(dataset_id: Optional[str]) -> Optional[pd.DataFrame]:
    """A stored dataset's rows, or None when the file is gone (older projects, cleaned-up uploads)."""
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT filename FROM datasets WHERE id = ?", (dataset_id,)).fetchone()
    try:
        return load_dataframe(row["filename"]) if row else None
    except DataPulseError:
        return None


def run_check(project: sqlite3.Row, name: str, path: Path, df: pd.DataFrame, use_ai: bool,
               actor: str, extra: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None, checked: Optional[pd.DataFrame] = None,
               combined: bool = False) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Check df against the baseline and save the run. `checked` is the part to check when it differs from
    the stored data (a unified table carries a _source column that isn't part of the standard). A combined table
    holds several sources' rows, so its row count isn't compared with the baseline's."""
    if not project["baseline_json"]:
        raise HTTPException(409, "Create a baseline dataset before running a check")
    cfg = project_config(project["config_yaml"])
    if combined:
        cfg = replace(cfg, row_count=replace(cfg.row_count, severity="off"))
    baseline = json.loads(project["baseline_json"])
    checked = df if checked is None else checked
    report = run_checks(baseline, checked, cfg, data_file=name, baseline_label=describe(baseline, "project baseline"),
                        config_label=CONFIG_LABEL, reference=dataset_rows(project["baseline_dataset_id"]))
    ai_note = explain_issues(report, cfg) if use_ai else ""
    doc = json.loads(render_json(report))
    dataset_id = record_dataset(project["id"], name, path, "candidate", df)
    run_id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]
    doc.update(run_id=run_id, ai_note=ai_note or None, markdown=render_markdown(report),
               charts=chart_data(baseline["profile"], checked), **(extra or {}))
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute("INSERT INTO runs (id, project_id, dataset_id, status, issue_count, report_json, created_at, "
                   "created_by) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)", (run_id, project["id"], dataset_id, doc["status"],
                                                                 len(doc["issues"]), json.dumps(doc), now_iso(), actor))
    return doc


def handle_data(project_id: str, name: str, path: Path, role: str, actor: str, use_ai: bool = False) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    try:
        project = project_row(project_id)
        df = load_upload(path)
        if role == "baseline":
            return save_baseline(project, name, path, df)
        return run_check(project, name, path, df, use_ai, actor)
    except HTTPException:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)  # rejected uploads are not kept
        raise


def ai_call(call):
    """Run an AI feature; configuration and upstream problems become readable HTTP errors."""
    try:
        return call()
    except DataPulseError as exc:
        raise HTTPException(503 if "environment variable" in str(exc) else 502, str(exc)) from exc
