"""AI repair agent: proposes fixes for a failed check, applies them to a copy, and proves they worked.

The agent can only use the safe repair operations defined here (no arbitrary code). Every operation it applies
is logged and replayed onto the original file to produce the fixed file, and the checks run again on the result,
so "9 issues -> 1" is measured, not claimed. Tool results contain counts and statistics, never data values.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from .agent import _tool
from .ai import create, get_client, json_format, safe_measured, slim_profile, text_of
from .checks import run_checks
from .config import Config
from .errors import DataPulseError
from .profile import profile_dataframe


class RepairError(ValueError):
    """An operation that can't be applied as asked; the message goes back to the agent."""


def _need(df: pd.DataFrame, column: str) -> pd.Series:
    if column not in df.columns:
        raise RepairError(f"There is no column named '{column}'.")
    return df[column]


def _numbers(s: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
    """Read the leading number from text like "5 units", "$1,234.50" or " 12 kg"; anything else becomes NaN."""
    found = s.astype("string").str.extract(r"^\s*[$€£]?\s*([-+]?\d[\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?)", expand=False)
    return pd.to_numeric(found.str.replace(",", "", regex=False), errors="coerce").astype("float64")


def rename_column(df, baseline, column: str, new_name: str):
    _need(df, column)
    if new_name in df.columns:
        raise RepairError(f"A column named '{new_name}' already exists.")
    return df.rename(columns={column: new_name}), f"Renamed '{column}' to '{new_name}'."


def scale_column(df, baseline, column: str, factor: float):
    s = _need(df, column)
    if not np.isfinite(factor) or factor == 0 or abs(factor) > 1e6:
        raise RepairError("The factor must be a non-zero number no larger than 1,000,000.")
    values = pd.to_numeric(s, errors="coerce")
    if values.notna().sum() == 0:
        raise RepairError(f"'{column}' has no numbers to scale; convert it to numbers first.")
    out = df.copy()
    out[column] = values * factor
    return out, f"Multiplied {int(values.notna().sum()):,} values in '{column}' by {factor:g}."


def convert_to_number(df, baseline, column: str):
    s = _need(df, column)
    converted = _numbers(s)
    lost = int((s.notna() & converted.isna()).sum())
    out = df.copy()
    out[column] = converted
    return out, (f"Converted '{column}' to numbers ({int(converted.notna().sum()):,} values)."
                 + (f" {lost:,} values could not be read as numbers and are now empty." if lost else ""))


def remove_duplicates(df, baseline, columns: List[str]):
    for c in columns:
        _need(df, c)
    out = df.drop_duplicates(subset=columns or None, keep="first").reset_index(drop=True)
    what = f"key {', '.join(columns)}" if columns else "whole rows"
    return out, f"Removed {len(df) - len(out):,} duplicate rows (by {what}), keeping the first of each."


def fill_missing(df, baseline, column: str, strategy: str):
    s = _need(df, column)
    missing = int(s.isna().sum())
    if not missing:
        raise RepairError(f"'{column}' has no empty values.")
    if strategy == "median":
        values = pd.to_numeric(s, errors="coerce")
        if values.notna().sum() == 0:
            raise RepairError(f"'{column}' has no numbers to take a median of.")
        fill = values.median()
    elif strategy == "most_common":
        if s.dropna().empty:
            raise RepairError(f"'{column}' has no values to choose from.")
        fill = s.mode(dropna=True).iloc[0]
    else:
        raise RepairError("strategy must be 'median' or 'most_common'.")
    out = df.copy()
    out[column] = s.fillna(fill)
    return out, f"Filled {missing:,} empty values in '{column}' with the column's {strategy.replace('_', ' ')} value."


def drop_rows_missing(df, baseline, column: str):
    s = _need(df, column)
    out = df[s.notna()].reset_index(drop=True)
    return out, f"Removed {len(df) - len(out):,} rows where '{column}' was empty."


def drop_column(df, baseline, column: str):
    _need(df, column)
    return df.drop(columns=[column]), f"Removed the column '{column}'."


def standardize_categories(df, baseline, column: str):
    """Map values that equal a baseline category apart from case/whitespace onto that category."""
    s = _need(df, column)
    known = (baseline["columns"].get(column) or {}).get("categories")
    if not known:
        raise RepairError(f"The baseline has no list of known values for '{column}'.")
    canonical = {str(k).strip().lower(): k for k in known}
    text = s.astype("string")
    mapped = text.map(lambda v: v if pd.isna(v) or v in known else canonical.get(v.strip().lower(), v))
    changed = int((mapped != text).fillna(False).sum())
    out = df.copy()
    out[column] = mapped.where(s.notna(), s)
    return out, f"Standardized {changed:,} values in '{column}' to the baseline's spelling."


OPERATIONS: Dict[str, Callable] = {f.__name__: f for f in (
    rename_column, scale_column, convert_to_number, remove_duplicates, fill_missing, drop_rows_missing,
    drop_column, standardize_categories)}


def apply_operations(df: pd.DataFrame, baseline_profile: Dict[str, Any], operations: List[Dict[str, Any]]):
    """Replay logged operations onto a file. Returns the fixed frame and one effect line per operation."""
    effects = []
    for op in operations:
        df, effect = OPERATIONS[op["operation"]](df, baseline_profile, **op["args"])
        effects.append(effect)
    return df, effects


def _counts(issues) -> Dict[str, int]:
    out = {"total": len(issues)}
    for sev in ("critical", "warning", "info"):
        out[sev] = sum(1 for i in issues if i.severity == sev)
    return out


# ---------- the agent ----------

REASON = {"reason": {"type": "string", "description": "One sentence: which issue this fixes and why this is the right fix"}}
COLUMN = {"column": {"type": "string", "description": "Exact column name"}}

TOOLS = [
    _tool("list_issues", "Run every check on the current working copy and list the issues that remain."),
    _tool("get_column_stats", "Baseline vs. working-copy statistics for one column (type, empty rate, distinct "
                              "values, numeric summary).", COLUMN),
    _tool("rename_column", "Rename a column, e.g. when the baseline's column arrives under a new name.",
          {**COLUMN, "new_name": {"type": "string"}, **REASON}),
    _tool("scale_column", "Multiply a numeric column by a factor, e.g. 0.01 to turn cents back into dollars.",
          {**COLUMN, "factor": {"type": "number"}, **REASON}),
    _tool("convert_to_number", "Turn a text column back into numbers by reading each value's leading number "
                                 "(handles units like '5 units', commas and currency signs).",
          {**COLUMN, **REASON}),
    _tool("remove_duplicates", "Remove duplicate rows, keeping the first. Give key columns, or [] for whole rows.",
          {"columns": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}}, **REASON}),
    _tool("fill_missing", "Fill empty values with the column's median or most common value. Only for columns "
                          "where a typical value is a safe stand-in; never for identifiers.",
          {**COLUMN, "strategy": {"type": "string", "enum": ["median", "most_common"]}, **REASON}),
    _tool("drop_rows_missing", "Remove rows where a column is empty.", {**COLUMN, **REASON}),
    _tool("drop_column", "Remove a column, e.g. one that is new and unexpected.", {**COLUMN, **REASON}),
    _tool("standardize_categories", "Map values that match a known baseline value apart from upper/lower case or "
                                    "spaces onto the baseline's spelling.", {**COLUMN, **REASON}),
    _tool("undo_last", "Undo the most recent repair."),
]

SYSTEM = """You are a data engineer repairing a batch of data that failed its quality checks. You work on a copy; \
the original file is never changed, and a person reviews your repairs before using the result.

Start with list_issues, look at the columns involved, then apply repairs one at a time with the repair tools and \
check the effect with list_issues. Undo anything that makes things worse.

Only repair what the evidence supports:
- A column that is ~100x or ~1000x its baseline scale is a unit change; scale it back.
- Numbers that arrived as text should be converted back.
- Duplicate keys should be removed.
- Values that differ from known values only by case or spaces should be standardized.
- Do not invent data. Missing columns cannot be recreated, and filling empty identifiers would be fabrication; \
leave those for a person and say so.
- New columns are usually harmless; remove one only if it clearly does not belong.

When you are done, summarize in one or two short sentences, and give each not_fixed reason in one sentence. You \
only ever see counts and statistics, never the data itself."""

SCHEMA = {
    "type": "object", "additionalProperties": False, "required": ["summary", "not_fixed", "warnings"],
    "properties": {
        "summary": {"type": "string"},
        "not_fixed": {"type": "array", "items": {
            "type": "object", "additionalProperties": False, "required": ["issue", "why"],
            "properties": {"issue": {"type": "string"}, "why": {"type": "string"}}}},
        "warnings": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
    },
}


MAP_SYSTEM = """You are mapping a new data source onto a project's standard format. The standard is the \
project's baseline: its column names, types and value scales. The source holds the same kind of data in its own \
shape - different column names, other units (cents instead of dollars), numbers stored as text, different \
capitalisation. Build a recipe with the tools so the source matches the standard.

Start with list_issues, then compare columns with get_column_stats: look up each unmatched standard column and \
each unmatched source column, and match them by type, empty rate, distinct values and numeric range, not by name \
alone. Then:
- rename source columns to their standard names;
- scale a column whose values are ~100x or ~1000x the standard's (after renaming it);
- convert numbers stored as text;
- standardize capitalisation and spacing of known values;
- remove duplicate rows by the standard's key columns when the standard is unique on them;
- remove source-only columns that have no counterpart in the standard.
Never guess: if the statistics don't clearly support a match, leave it and list it under not_fixed. The steps you \
apply are replayed automatically on every future load once a person approves them. You only ever see counts and \
statistics, never the data itself."""

MAP_TOOLS = [t for t in TOOLS if t["name"] not in ("fill_missing", "drop_rows_missing")]


def _repair_intro(session, before) -> str:
    return (f"This batch has {len(session.original):,} rows and failed with {len(before.issues)} issue(s). "
            "Repair what can safely be repaired.")


def _map_intro(session, before) -> str:
    standard = list(session.baseline["profile"]["columns"])
    return (f"Standard columns: {json.dumps(standard)}\n"
            f"Source columns (after {len(session.operations)} automatic step(s)): {json.dumps(list(session.working.columns))}\n"
            f"The source has {len(session.original):,} rows and currently shows {len(session.check().issues)} issue(s) "
            "against the standard. Map it.")


MODES = {"repair": (SYSTEM, TOOLS, _repair_intro), "map": (MAP_SYSTEM, MAP_TOOLS, _map_intro)}


@dataclass
class RepairSession:
    original: pd.DataFrame
    baseline: Dict[str, Any]  # a baseline file's contents (build_baseline output)
    cfg: Config
    reference: Optional[pd.DataFrame] = None  # the baseline's rows, so the ML drift check runs too
    operations: List[Dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)

    allowed: Optional[set] = None  # tool names the agent may use; None means all

    def __post_init__(self):
        self.working, _ = apply_operations(self.original, self.baseline["profile"], self.operations)

    def check(self, df: Optional[pd.DataFrame] = None):
        return run_checks(self.baseline, self.working if df is None else df, self.cfg, reference=self.reference)

    def list_issues(self) -> Any:
        report = self.check()
        return {"rows": len(self.working), "issues": [
            {"check": i.check, "severity": i.severity, "title": i.title, "columns": i.columns,
             "measured": safe_measured(i.measured)} for i in report.issues]}

    def get_column_stats(self, column: str) -> Any:
        current = slim_profile(profile_dataframe(self.working, self.cfg.max_categories))
        base = slim_profile(self.baseline["profile"])
        return {"column": column, "baseline": base["columns"].get(column, "not present"),
                "working_copy": current["columns"].get(column, "not present")}

    def undo_last(self) -> Any:
        if not self.operations:
            raise RepairError("There is nothing to undo.")
        undone = self.operations.pop()
        self.working, _ = apply_operations(self.original, self.baseline["profile"], self.operations)
        return f"Undid {undone['operation']} on {undone['args']}."

    def repair(self, name: str, args: Dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
        reason = args.pop("reason", "")
        self.working, effect = OPERATIONS[name](self.working, self.baseline["profile"], **args)
        self.operations.append({"operation": name, "args": args, "reason": reason, "effect": effect})
        return {"effect": effect, "issues_remaining": _counts(self.check().issues)}

    def call(self, name: str, args: Dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
        if self.allowed is not None and name not in self.allowed:
            raise RepairError(f"{name} is not available for this task.")
        if name in OPERATIONS:
            return self.repair(name, dict(args))
        if name in ("list_issues", "get_column_stats", "undo_last"):
            return getattr(self, name)(**args)
        raise RepairError(f"Unknown tool {name}")


def run_repair(session: RepairSession, client=None, max_turns: int = 16,
               on_step: Optional[Callable[[str, Dict[str, Any]], None]] = None, mode: str = "repair") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Let the agent repair a copy, then replay its operations onto the original and re-run every check.

    mode "map" uses the same loop to translate a new source into the project's standard format (see unify.py).
    """
    cfg = session.cfg
    client = client or get_client(cfg)
    system, tools, intro = MODES[mode]
    session.allowed = {t["name"] for t in tools}
    before = session.check(session.original)
    messages: List[Dict[str, Any]] = [{"role": "user", "content": intro(session, before)}]
    for _ in range(max_turns):
        response = create(client, cfg, system=system, tools=tools, messages=messages,
                          output_config={**json_format(SCHEMA), "effort": "medium"})
        messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": response.content})
        if response.stop_reason == "pause_turn":
            continue
        calls = [b for b in response.content if b.type == "tool_use"]
        if not calls:
            try:
                result = json.loads(text_of(response))
            except ValueError as e:
                raise DataPulseError(f"The repair summary was not valid JSON: {e}")
            break
        results = []
        for c in calls:
            if on_step:
                on_step(c.name, c.input)
            try:
                content, error = json.dumps(session.call(c.name, c.input), default=str), False
            except (RepairError, TypeError) as e:
                content, error = str(e), True
            results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": c.id, "content": content, "is_error": error})
        messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
    else:
        raise DataPulseError(f"The AI agent did not finish within {max_turns} steps.")

    fixed, effects = apply_operations(session.original, session.baseline["profile"], session.operations)
    after = session.check(fixed)
    return {
        **result,
        "operations": [{**op, "effect": eff} for op, eff in zip(session.operations, effects)],
        "before": _counts(before.issues),
        "after": {**_counts(after.issues), "remaining": [i.title for i in after.issues]},
        "rows": {"before": len(session.original), "after": len(fixed)},
        "model": cfg.ai.model,
        "fixed_frame": fixed,  # removed by callers before serializing
    }

"""Git and GitHub helpers: baseline-from-ref and PR comments (stdlib only)."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import subprocess
import urllib.error
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Mapping, Optional, Tuple

from .errors import DataPulseError
from .report import MARKER

MAX_COMMENT = 60000


def _git(args, cwd=None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
    try:
        return subprocess.run(["git", *args], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=cwd)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        raise DataPulseError("git is not installed, but --baseline-ref needs it.")


def _repo_relative(path):
    top = _git(["rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"])
    if top.returncode != 0:
        raise DataPulseError("Reading files from a git ref requires running inside a git repository.")
    root = Path(top.stdout.strip())
    try:
        return root, Path(path).resolve().relative_to(root.resolve())
    except ValueError:
        raise DataPulseError(f"{path} is outside the git repository at {root}.")


def ref_exists(ref: str) -> bool:
    return _git(["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", f"{ref}^{{commit}}"]).returncode == 0


def path_exists_at_ref(ref: str, path) -> bool:
    root, rel = _repo_relative(path)
    return _git(["cat-file", "-e", f"{ref}:{rel.as_posix()}"], cwd=root).returncode == 0


def read_file_at_ref(ref: str, path) -> str:
    root, rel = _repo_relative(path)
    res = _git(["show", f"{ref}:{rel.as_posix()}"], cwd=root)
    if res.returncode != 0:
        raise DataPulseError(
            f"Could not read '{rel.as_posix()}' at git ref '{ref}': {res.stderr.strip()}. "
            "Is the baseline committed on that branch, and has the ref been fetched "
            "(git fetch origin <branch>)?")
    return res.stdout


def _request(method: str, url: str, token: str, payload: Optional[dict] = None) -> Any:
    headers = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}", "Accept": "application/vnd.github+json",
               "X-GitHub-Api-Version": "2022-11-28", "User-Agent": "datapulse"}
    data = json.dumps(payload).encode() if payload is not None else None
    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30) as resp:
            body = resp.read().decode()
            return json.loads(body) if body else None
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        hint = " (fork PRs get a read-only token; the report is still in the job summary)" if e.code == 403 else ""
        raise DataPulseError(f"GitHub API {method} failed with HTTP {e.code}{hint}")
    except (urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError) as e:
        raise DataPulseError(f"GitHub API request failed: {e}")


def detect_pr_context(env: Mapping[str, str]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], Optional[int]]:
    repo = env.get("GITHUB_REPOSITORY")
    pr = None
    event = env.get("GITHUB_EVENT_PATH")
    if event and Path(event).exists():
        try:
            data = json.loads(Path(event).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
            pr = (data.get("pull_request") or {}).get("number") or data.get("number")
        except (OSError, ValueError):
            pass
    return repo, pr


def upsert_pr_comment(body: str, repo: str, pr: int, token: str, api_url: str = "https://api.github.com") -> str:
    if MARKER not in body:
        body = MARKER + "\n" + body
    if len(body) > MAX_COMMENT:
        body = body[:MAX_COMMENT] + "\n\n_...report truncated; see the Markdown report artifact or job summary._"
    base = f"{api_url.rstrip('/')}/repos/{repo}"
    existing = None
    page = 1
    while existing is None:
        comments = _request("GET", f"{base}/issues/{pr}/comments?per_page=100&page={page}", token) or []
        existing = next((c for c in comments if MARKER in (c.get("body") or "")), None)
        if len(comments) < 100:
            break
        page += 1
    if existing:
        _request("PATCH", f"{base}/issues/comments/{existing['id']}", token, {"body": body})
        return "updated"
    _request("POST", f"{base}/issues/{pr}/comments", token, {"body": body})
    return "created"


def default_api_url() -> str:
    return os.environ.get("GITHUB_API_URL", "https://api.github.com")

"""Reading CSV / Parquet / JSON files with clear errors."""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from .errors import DataPulseError

SUPPORTED = (".csv", ".parquet", ".pq", ".json", ".jsonl", ".ndjson", ".xlsx", ".xls")
JSON_LINES = (".jsonl", ".ndjson")
EXCEL = (".xlsx", ".xls")
# Unstructured inputs: the document unifier extracts records from these (see datapulse.unifier.documents).
DOCUMENTS = (".pdf", ".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".webp", ".gif", ".docx", ".md", ".markdown", ".txt",
             ".eml", ".html", ".htm")
ARCHIVES = (".zip",)
INPUTS = SUPPORTED + DOCUMENTS + ARCHIVES


def load_dataframe(path) -> pd.DataFrame:
    p = Path(path)
    if not p.exists():
        raise DataPulseError(f"Data file not found: {p}")
    if not p.is_file():
        raise DataPulseError(f"Not a file: {p}")
    ext = p.suffix.lower()
    if ext not in SUPPORTED:
        raise DataPulseError(
            f"Unsupported file type '{p.suffix}' for {p}. "
            "Supported: .csv, .parquet, .json, .jsonl, .xlsx"
        )
    try:
        if ext == ".csv":
            df = pd.read_csv(p)
        elif ext == ".json" or ext in JSON_LINES:  # an array of row objects, or one object per line
            df = pd.read_json(p, orient="records", lines=ext in JSON_LINES, convert_dates=False)
        elif ext in EXCEL:
            df = pd.read_excel(p)  # first sheet; needs openpyxl for .xlsx
        else:
            df = pd.read_parquet(p)
    except pd.errors.EmptyDataError:
        raise DataPulseError(f"{p} is empty (no header row).")
    except ImportError as e:
        hint = "openpyxl" if ext in EXCEL else "pyarrow"
        raise DataPulseError(f"Missing dependency to read {p}: {e}. Try: pip install {hint}")
    except Exception as e:  # corrupt files, bad encodings, ragged CSV rows...
        raise DataPulseError(f"Could not read {p} as {ext[1:]}: {e}")
    return normalize_frame(df, str(p))


def normalize_frame(df: pd.DataFrame, label: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Shared clean-up for every way data arrives (files, databases, links): text column names, no duplicates,
    nested values (lists/dicts) as text so they can be compared."""
    if df.shape[1] == 0:
        raise DataPulseError(f"{label} has no columns.")
    df.columns = [str(c) for c in df.columns]
    if df.columns.duplicated().any():
        dupes = sorted(set(df.columns[df.columns.duplicated()]))
        raise DataPulseError(f"{label} has duplicate column names: {dupes}")
    for col in df.columns:
        s = df[col]
        if s.dtype == object and s.map(lambda v: isinstance(v, (list, dict, set, tuple, np.ndarray))).any():
            df[col] = s.map(lambda v: v if v is None or (isinstance(v, float) and np.isnan(v)) else str(v))
    return df


def file_sha256(path) -> str:
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(1 << 20), b""):
            h.update(chunk)
    return h.hexdigest()

FILE [77/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\src\datapulse\ml.py

"""Learned drift detection: train a classifier to tell baseline rows from current rows.

If a model can't tell the two files apart (ROC AUC near 0.5) they come from the same distribution. If it can,
the data changed, and the model's feature importances say which columns gave it away. Unlike the per-column
checks this also catches changes in how columns relate to each other. Runs locally; no data leaves the machine.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Sequence

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api import types as pt

from .profile import column_kind

MIN_ROWS = 50
MAX_CATEGORIES = 50


def _skip_reason(base: pd.Series, cur: pd.Series) -> Optional[str]:
    """Columns that differ between any two batches by design would make every batch look drifted."""
    both = pd.concat([base, cur], ignore_index=True).dropna()
    if both.empty:
        return "empty"
    kind = column_kind(base)
    if kind == "datetime":
        return "timestamp"
    if kind == "string":
        sample = both.astype(str).head(200)
        if pd.to_datetime(sample, errors="coerce", format="mixed").notna().mean() > 0.9:
            return "timestamp"
        if both.nunique() > 0.5 * len(both):
            return "identifier"
    elif kind == "integer" and base.nunique() == base.notna().sum() > 1:
        return "identifier"
    return None


def _encode(base: pd.Series, cur: pd.Series) -> Optional[np.ndarray]:
    """One numeric feature per column for both files stacked (baseline rows first); NaN kept as -1 / median."""
    both = pd.concat([base, cur], ignore_index=True)
    if column_kind(base) in ("integer", "float", "boolean") and column_kind(cur) in ("integer", "float", "boolean"):
        values = pd.to_numeric(both, errors="coerce").astype(float)
        if not np.isfinite(values).any():
            return None
        return np.column_stack([values.fillna(values.median()), values.isna()])
    text = both.astype("string")
    top = text.value_counts().index[:MAX_CATEGORIES]
    codes = pd.Categorical(text.where(text.isin(top), "__other__")).codes.astype(float)  # NaN -> -1
    return codes.reshape(-1, 1)


def drift_model(base: pd.DataFrame, cur: pd.DataFrame, exclude: Sequence[str] = (), max_rows: int = 5000,
                seed: int = 0) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Fit a random forest to separate baseline from current rows; report out-of-bag AUC and column drivers."""
    from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier
    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score

    n = min(len(base), len(cur), max_rows)
    result: Dict[str, Any] = {"auc": None, "drivers": [], "skipped": {}, "rows_per_side": n}
    if n < MIN_ROWS:
        result["reason"] = f"needs at least {MIN_ROWS} rows in each file"
        return result
    base = base.sample(n, random_state=seed) if len(base) > n else base
    cur = cur.sample(n, random_state=seed) if len(cur) > n else cur

    blocks, owners = [], []
    for col in (c for c in base.columns if c in cur.columns):
        reason = "excluded in config" if col in exclude else _skip_reason(base[col], cur[col])
        encoded = None if reason else _encode(base[col], cur[col])
        if encoded is None:
            result["skipped"][col] = reason or "no usable values"
            continue
        blocks.append(encoded)
        owners += [col] * encoded.shape[1]
    if not blocks:
        result["reason"] = "no comparable columns"
        return result

    X = np.hstack(blocks)
    y = np.r_[np.zeros(n), np.ones(n)]
    model = RandomForestClassifier(n_estimators=150, min_samples_leaf=5, max_features="sqrt", oob_score=True,
                                   random_state=seed, n_jobs=1).fit(X, y)
    oob = np.nan_to_num(model.oob_decision_function_[:, 1], nan=0.5)
    # Below 0.5 is noise (exact duplicate rows in both files predict each other's label), not "less than no drift".
    result["auc"] = round(max(0.5, float(roc_auc_score(y, oob))), 4)
    weights: Dict[str, float] = {}
    for col, w in zip(owners, model.feature_importances_):
        weights[col] = weights.get(col, 0.0) + float(w)
    total = sum(weights.values()) or 1.0
    result["drivers"] = sorted(([c, round(w / total, 3)] for c, w in weights.items()), key=lambda d: -d[1])
    return result


def top_drivers(drivers: List[List[Any]], min_share: float = 0.1, limit: int = 3) -> List[List[Any]]:
    return [d for d in drivers if d[1] >= min_share][:limit] or drivers[:1]

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional

from .config import LEVEL


@dataclass
class Issue:
    check: str
    severity: str
    title: str
    detail: str  # measured facts only
    columns: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    measured: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)  # aggregate scalars only
    examples: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # raw data values; never sent to AI
    explanation: str = ""  # deterministic, suggested (not verified)
    consumers: List[Dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
    ai_explanation: Optional[str] = None
    code_refs: List[Dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)  # detected by text search (heuristic)
    code_refs_more: int = 0


@dataclass
class CheckReport:
    data_file: str
    baseline_label: str
    baseline_rows: int
    current_rows: int
    current_columns: int
    issues: List[Issue]
    checks_run: List[str]
    fail_on: str
    max_examples: int = 5
    config_label: str = "defaults"
    triage: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None  # AI triage agent output (agent.run_triage), when requested

    @property
    def failed(self) -> bool:
        if self.fail_on == "never":
            return False
        threshold = LEVEL[self.fail_on]
        return any(LEVEL[i.severity] >= threshold for i in self.issues)

    @property
    def exit_code(self) -> int:
        return 1 if self.failed else 0

    def counts(self) -> Dict[str, int]:
        return {s: sum(1 for i in self.issues if i.severity == s) for s in ("critical", "warning", "info")}

    @property
    def schema_issues(self) -> List[Issue]:
        return [i for i in self.issues if i.check.startswith("schema.")]

    @property
    def schema_changed(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.schema_issues)

    @property
    def affected_consumers(self) -> List[str]:
        return sorted({c["name"] for i in self.issues for c in i.consumers})

    @property
    def passed_checks(self) -> List[str]:
        failing = {i.check.split(".")[0] for i in self.issues}
        return [c for c in self.checks_run if c not in failing]

"""Profiling a DataFrame into JSON-serialisable aggregate statistics."""
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api import types as pt

NUMERIC_KINDS = ("integer", "float")
QUANTILES = {"p01": 0.01, "p25": 0.25, "p50": 0.5, "p75": 0.75, "p99": 0.99}


def column_kind(s: pd.Series) -> str:
    """Logical type: boolean | datetime | integer | float | string.

    A float column whose non-null values are all whole numbers is reported as
    "integer", because pandas turns integer CSV columns into floats as soon as
    a null appears and that should not look like a type change.
    """
    if pt.is_bool_dtype(s):
        return "boolean"
    if pt.is_datetime64_any_dtype(s):
        return "datetime"
    if pt.is_integer_dtype(s):
        return "integer"
    if pt.is_float_dtype(s):
        values = s.dropna().to_numpy(dtype=float)
        if values.size and np.isfinite(values).all() and np.all(values == np.floor(values)):
            return "integer"
        return "float"
    return "string"


def bin_proportions(values, edges: List[float]) -> List[float]:
    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=float)
    if values.size == 0:
        return [0.0] * (len(edges) + 1)
    idx = np.searchsorted(np.asarray(edges, dtype=float), values, side="right")
    counts = np.bincount(idx, minlength=len(edges) + 1)
    return (counts / values.size).tolist()


def numeric_values(s: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
    v = pd.to_numeric(s, errors="coerce").astype(float)
    return v[np.isfinite(v)]


def numeric_profile(s: pd.Series) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
    v = numeric_values(s)
    if v.empty:
        return None
    edges = sorted({float(x) for x in v.quantile(np.linspace(0.1, 0.9, 9)).tolist()})
    out: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "min": float(v.min()), "max": float(v.max()),
        "mean": float(v.mean()), "std": float(v.std(ddof=0)),
    }
    for name, q in QUANTILES.items():
        out[name] = float(v.quantile(q))
    out["bin_edges"] = edges
    out["bin_props"] = bin_proportions(v.to_numpy(), edges)
    return out


def profile_dataframe(df: pd.DataFrame, max_categories: int = 50) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    n = len(df)
    columns: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    for name in df.columns:
        s = df[name]
        kind = column_kind(s)
        nulls = int(s.isna().sum())
        info: Dict[str, Any] = {
            "dtype": str(s.dtype), "kind": kind,
            "null_count": nulls, "null_rate": (nulls / n) if n else 0.0,
            "unique_count": int(s.nunique(dropna=True)),
        }
        if kind in NUMERIC_KINDS:
            num = numeric_profile(s)
            if num:
                info["numeric"] = num
        elif kind == "string" and 0 < info["unique_count"] <= max_categories and info["unique_count"] <= 0.5 * n:
            counts = s.dropna().astype(str).value_counts()
            info["categories"] = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in counts.items()}
        columns[str(name)] = info
    return {"rows": n, "duplicate_rows": int(df.duplicated().sum()) if n else 0, "columns": columns}


def chart_data(baseline_profile: Dict[str, Any], df: pd.DataFrame) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Baseline vs. current per column, for before/after charts: empty-value rates, and for numeric columns the
    share of values in each of the baseline's histogram buckets (so both sides use the same buckets)."""
    out: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    for col, b in baseline_profile["columns"].items():
        present = col in df.columns
        entry: Dict[str, Any] = {"null_rate": [round(b["null_rate"], 4),
                                               round(float(df[col].isna().mean()), 4) if present and len(df) else None]}
        bn = b.get("numeric")
        values = numeric_values(df[col]) if present and bn else None
        if bn and values is not None and not values.empty:
            entry["hist"] = {"edges": bn["bin_edges"], "baseline": [round(p, 4) for p in bn["bin_props"]],
                             "current": [round(p, 4) for p in bin_proportions(values.to_numpy(), bn["bin_edges"])]}
        out[col] = entry
    return out

"""Terminal and Markdown reports."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from typing import Any

from rich.console import Console
from rich.text import Text

from . import __version__
from .models import CheckReport, Issue

MARKER = "<!-- datapulse-report -->"
STYLE = {"critical": "bold red", "warning": "yellow", "info": "cyan"}
EMOJI = {"critical": "🔴", "warning": "🟡", "info": "🔵"}


def _fmt(v: Any) -> str:
    if v is None:
        return "n/a"
    if isinstance(v, bool):
        return "yes" if v else "no"
    if isinstance(v, int):
        return f"{v:,}"
    if isinstance(v, float):
        return f"{v:.4g}"
    return str(v)


def _label(key: str) -> str:
    return key.replace("_", " ")


def _consumer_line(c: dict) -> str:
    meta = c["type"] + (f", owner: {c['owner']}" if c["owner"] else "")
    if c["dataset_wide"]:
        return f"{c['name']} ({meta}): reads this file; the issue is dataset-wide"
    return f"{c['name']} ({meta}): uses {', '.join(c['columns'])}"


def _code_ref_line(r: dict, md: bool = False) -> str:
    q = (lambda x: f"`{x}`") if md else (lambda x: f"'{x}'")
    what = "; ".join(f"{q(c)} line {', '.join(map(str, ls))}" for c, ls in r["columns"].items())
    via = r["via"]
    return f"{q(r['file']) if md else r['file']} ({r['language']}): {what or f'reads this dataset (matched {via})'}"


def _summary_line(report: CheckReport) -> str:
    return ", ".join(f"{n} {s}" for s, n in report.counts().items() if n) or "no issues"


def render_terminal(report: CheckReport, console: Console) -> None:
    def out(text: str = "", style: str = "") -> None:
        console.print(Text(text, style=style))

    head = Text("DataPulse check: ", style="bold")
    head.append("FAILED" if report.failed else "PASSED", style="bold red" if report.failed else "bold green")
    console.print(head)
    out(f"Data file: {report.data_file}")
    out(f"Baseline:  {report.baseline_label}")
    out(f"Config:    {report.config_label}")
    out(f"Rows:      {report.baseline_rows:,} (baseline) -> {report.current_rows:,} (current), "
        f"{report.current_columns} columns")
    out(f"Issues:    {_summary_line(report)}  (fail_on: {report.fail_on})")
    for n, issue in enumerate(report.issues, 1):
        out()
        t = Text(f"[{issue.severity.upper()}] ", style=STYLE[issue.severity])
        t.append(f"{n}. {issue.title}", style="bold")
        t.append(f"   ({issue.check})", style="dim")
        console.print(t)
        out("  Measured: " + issue.detail)
        for k, v in issue.measured.items():
            out(f"    - {_label(k)}: {_fmt(v)}", "dim")
        if issue.examples:
            out("  Examples from the data: " + "; ".join(issue.examples), "dim")
        out("  Possible explanation (suggested, not verified): " + issue.explanation, "italic")
        if issue.ai_explanation:
            out("  AI-generated explanation (suggested, not verified): " + issue.ai_explanation, "italic magenta")
        if issue.code_refs:
            out("  Code that references this (detected by text search; heuristic, not verified):")
            for r in issue.code_refs:
                out("    - " + _code_ref_line(r))
            if issue.code_refs_more:
                out(f"    ...and {issue.code_refs_more} more file(s)", "dim")
        if issue.consumers:
            out("  Downstream consumers that may be affected:")
            for c in issue.consumers:
                out("    * " + _consumer_line(c))
        elif issue.columns:
            out("  Downstream consumers: none of the configured consumers use this column.", "dim")
    out()
    if report.passed_checks:
        out("Passed checks: " + ", ".join(report.passed_checks), "green")
    if report.triage:
        out()
        for line in triage_lines(report.triage):
            out(line, "magenta" if line.startswith("AI triage") else "")


def _md(s: str) -> str:
    return str(s).replace("|", "\\|").replace("\n", " ")


def render_markdown(report: CheckReport) -> str:
    counts = report.counts()
    icon = "❌" if report.failed else ("⚠️" if report.issues else "✅")
    title = ("DataPulse: checks failed" if report.failed else
             "DataPulse: passed with issues" if report.issues else "DataPulse: all checks passed")
    L = [MARKER, f"## {icon} {title}", "",
         "| | |", "|---|---|",
         f"| Data file | `{report.data_file}` |",
         f"| Baseline | {_md(report.baseline_label)} |",
         f"| Config | {_md(report.config_label)} |",
         f"| Rows | {report.baseline_rows:,} → {report.current_rows:,} ({report.current_columns} columns) |",
         f"| Issues | {_summary_line(report)} |",
         f"| CI gate | fails on `{report.fail_on}` → **{'FAILED' if report.failed else 'PASSED'}** |", ""]
    L += ["### Pull request impact", ""]
    if report.schema_issues:
        for i in report.schema_issues:
            cols = ", ".join(f"`{c}`" for c in i.columns)
            L.append(f"- **{i.severity.upper()}** {cols}: {_md(i.title)}")
    else:
        L.append("No schema changes detected.")
    names = report.affected_consumers
    L += ["", "**Affected consumers:** " + (", ".join(f"`{n}`" for n in names) if names else "none mapped"), ""]
    if report.issues:
        L += ["### Issues", "", "| Severity | Check | Column | Measured | Affected consumers |", "|---|---|---|---|---|"]
        for i in report.issues:
            cols = ", ".join(f"`{c}`" for c in i.columns) or "(dataset)"
            cons = ", ".join(c["name"] for c in i.consumers) or "none"
            L.append(f"| {EMOJI[i.severity]} {i.severity} | `{i.check}` | {cols} | {_md(i.detail)} | {_md(cons)} |")
        L += ["", "### Details", ""]
        for n, i in enumerate(report.issues, 1):
            L += [f"#### {EMOJI[i.severity]} {n}. {i.title}", "", "**Measured from the data**", "", f"{i.detail}", ""]
            L += [f"- {_label(k)}: `{_fmt(v)}`" for k, v in i.measured.items()]
            if i.examples:
                L += ["", "Examples from the data: " + "; ".join(f"`{e}`" for e in i.examples)]
            L += ["", "**Possible explanation** _(suggested, not verified)_", "", f"{i.explanation}", ""]
            if i.ai_explanation:
                L += ["**AI-generated explanation** _(suggested, not verified)_", "", i.ai_explanation, ""]
            if i.code_refs:
                L += ["**Code that references this** _(detected by text search; heuristic, not verified)_", ""]
                L += [f"- {_code_ref_line(r, md=True)}" for r in i.code_refs]
                if i.code_refs_more:
                    L.append(f"- _...and {i.code_refs_more} more file(s)_")
                L.append("")
            if i.consumers:
                L += ["**Downstream consumers that may be affected**", ""]
                L += [f"- {_consumer_line(c)}" for c in i.consumers]
                L.append("")
            elif i.columns:
                L += ["_No configured consumer uses this column._", ""]
    if report.passed_checks:
        L += ["### Checks that passed", "", ", ".join(f"`{c}`" for c in report.passed_checks), ""]
    if report.triage:
        t = report.triage
        L += ["### AI triage _(AI-generated from aggregate statistics; verify before acting)_", "",
              f"**{_md(t['headline'])}**", "", f"Verdict: **{VERDICT[t['verdict']]}**. {t['verdict_reason']}", ""]
        L += ["**Likely causes**", ""] + [f"- ({c['confidence']}) {c['hypothesis']} _Evidence: {c['evidence']}_"
                                          for c in t["root_causes"]] + [""]
        L += ["**Next steps**", ""] + [f"- [ ] {a['step']} ({a['owner']})" for a in t["actions"]] + [""]
        if t["notify"]:
            L += ["**Notify**", ""] + [f"- {n['who']}: {n['why']}" for n in t["notify"]] + [""]
        L += ["**Message to send**", "", "> " + t["message"].replace("\n", "\n> "), ""]
    L += ["---", "_Numbers above are measured from aggregate profile statistics. Explanations are suggestions "
          "and are not verified. No dataset rows were uploaded anywhere._", ""]
    return "\n".join(L)


VERDICT = {"block": "Block this batch", "investigate": "Investigate before shipping", "safe_to_ship": "Safe to ship"}


def triage_lines(t: dict) -> list:
    lines = [f"AI triage (AI-generated, verify before acting): {t['headline']}",
             f"  Verdict: {VERDICT[t['verdict']]}. {t['verdict_reason']}", "  Likely causes:"]
    lines += [f"    - ({c['confidence']}) {c['hypothesis']}" for c in t["root_causes"]]
    lines += ["  Next steps:"] + [f"    [ ] {a['step']} ({a['owner']})" for a in t["actions"]]
    lines += ["  Notify:"] + [f"    - {n['who']}: {n['why']}" for n in t["notify"]] if t["notify"] else []
    return lines + ["  Message to send:"] + ["    " + ln for ln in t["message"].splitlines()]


def render_json(report: CheckReport) -> str:
    """Machine-readable report for tooling in any language (stable, versioned schema)."""
    doc = {
        "schema_version": 1,
        "tool": "datapulse",
        "version": __version__,
        "status": "failed" if report.failed else "passed",
        "exit_code": report.exit_code,
        "fail_on": report.fail_on,
        "data_file": report.data_file,
        "baseline": report.baseline_label,
        "config": report.config_label,
        "schema_changed": report.schema_changed,
        "affected_consumers": report.affected_consumers,
        "rows": {"baseline": report.baseline_rows, "current": report.current_rows},
        "columns_current": report.current_columns,
        "counts": report.counts(),
        "checks_run": report.checks_run,
        "passed_checks": report.passed_checks,
        "triage": report.triage,
        "issues": [
            {"check": i.check, "severity": i.severity, "title": i.title, "columns": i.columns,
             "measured_summary": i.detail, "measured": i.measured, "examples": i.examples,
             "suggested_explanation": i.explanation, "ai_explanation": i.ai_explanation,
             "consumers": i.consumers, "code_references": i.code_refs}
            for i in report.issues
        ],
    }
    return json.dumps(doc, indent=2, default=str) + "\n"


def action_outputs(doc: dict) -> dict:
    """Single-line values for GitHub Actions step outputs, derived from a JSON report.

    Values are sanitized (no control characters or newlines) so they can never inject extra
    `key=value` lines into $GITHUB_OUTPUT, even if a consumer name in the config is hostile.
    """
    def clean(value: Any) -> str:
        return " ".join("".join(ch if ch.isprintable() else " " for ch in str(value)).split())

    issues = doc.get("issues", [])
    consumers = sorted({str(c.get("name", "")) for i in issues for c in i.get("consumers", [])})
    return {
        "status": clean(doc.get("status", "error")),
        "issue-count": str(len(issues)),
        "schema-changed": "true" if any(str(i.get("check", "")).startswith("schema.") for i in issues) else "false",
        "affected-consumers": clean(", ".join(consumers)),
    }

"""Sample sources for demos: the same orders stored by three systems, each in its own format."""
from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from contextlib import closing
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Tuple

import pandas as pd


def warehouse(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """The standard: the data warehouse's own format."""
    return df


def store_system(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """A point-of-sale database: other column names, amounts in cents, upper-case statuses, an extra column."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"OrderID": df.order_id + 50_000, "Customer ID": df.customer_id, "STATUS": df.status.str.upper(),
                         "amount_cents": (df.amount * 100).round(), "Quantity": df.quantity, "Country": df.country,
                         "Created_At": df.created_at, "warehouse": "W1"})


def online_shop(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """A web shop: different names throughout, prices as text like "$61.31", title-case statuses."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"order_number": df.order_id + 90_000, "customer": df.customer_id, "state": df.status.str.title(),
                         "total": df.amount.map(lambda v: f"${v:,.2f}"), "qty": df.quantity, "country_code": df.country,
                         "ordered_at": df.created_at})


# (source name, file, table, shape)
SOURCES: List[Tuple[str, str, str, Callable[[pd.DataFrame], pd.DataFrame]]] = [
    ("Warehouse database", "warehouse.db", "orders", warehouse),
    ("Store system", "store_system.db", "store_orders", store_system),
    ("Online shop", "online_shop.db", "web_orders", online_shop),
]


def write_samples(orders: pd.DataFrame, folder: Path) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
    """Write each sample source as a SQLite database; returns name + settings for each."""
    folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    out = []
    for name, file, table, shape in SOURCES:
        path = folder / file
        with closing(sqlite3.connect(path)) as db, db:
            shape(orders).to_sql(table, db, index=False, if_exists="replace")
        out.append({"name": name, "path": str(path), "query": table})
    return out

"""Language-agnostic discovery of code that reads a dataset and references its columns.

This is a heuristic *text search*, not a parser or data-flow analysis. Results are leads to review,
never verified facts: dynamic column names are missed and generic names can produce false positives.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import bisect
import os
import re
from collections import Counter
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Iterable, List, Sequence

LANGUAGES = {
    ".py": "Python", ".ipynb": "Jupyter Notebook",
    ".js": "JavaScript", ".jsx": "JavaScript", ".mjs": "JavaScript", ".cjs": "JavaScript",
    ".ts": "TypeScript", ".tsx": "TypeScript",
    ".java": "Java", ".kt": "Kotlin", ".kts": "Kotlin", ".scala": "Scala",
    ".go": "Go", ".rs": "Rust", ".rb": "Ruby", ".r": "R", ".cs": "C#", ".php": "PHP",
    ".sql": "SQL", ".jl": "Julia", ".swift": "Swift",
}
MANIFESTS = {
    "package.json": "Node.js (JavaScript/TypeScript)", "tsconfig.json": "TypeScript", "go.mod": "Go",
    "Cargo.toml": "Rust", "pom.xml": "Java (Maven)", "build.gradle": "JVM (Gradle)",
    "build.gradle.kts": "JVM (Gradle)", "build.sbt": "Scala (sbt)", "Gemfile": "Ruby",
    "composer.json": "PHP", "pyproject.toml": "Python", "requirements.txt": "Python", "setup.py": "Python",
    "Project.toml": "Julia", "Package.swift": "Swift", "DESCRIPTION": "R", "dbt_project.yml": "dbt (SQL)",
}
DEFAULT_EXCLUDE = {"node_modules", "venv", "env", "target", "build", "dist", "__pycache__", "vendor", "obj"}
MAX_FILES = 20000
MAX_BYTES = 1_000_000
MAX_LINES = 3


@dataclass
class CodeRef:
    file: str
    language: str
    reads_dataset: bool
    via: str
    columns: Dict[str, List[int]] = field(default_factory=dict)

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {"file": self.file, "language": self.language, "reads_dataset": self.reads_dataset,
                "via": self.via, "columns": self.columns}


@dataclass
class ScanResult:
    root: str
    ecosystems: List[str]
    languages: Dict[str, int]
    files_scanned: int
    truncated: bool
    refs: List[CodeRef]


def language_slug(language: str) -> str:
    return language.lower().replace("#", "sharp").replace("+", "p").replace(" ", "-")


def _variants(col: str) -> List[str]:
    """camelCase / PascalCase spellings of a column, for Java, Go, C#, JS and similar code."""
    parts = [p for p in re.split(r"[_\-\s]+", col) if p]
    if not parts:
        return []
    camel = parts[0][:1].lower() + parts[0][1:] + "".join(p[:1].upper() + p[1:] for p in parts[1:])
    pascal = "".join(p[:1].upper() + p[1:] for p in parts)
    return [v for v in dict.fromkeys((camel, pascal)) if v != col and len(v) >= 3 and re.fullmatch(r"\w+", v)]


_CACHE: Dict[tuple, "re.Pattern[str]"] = {}


def _column_regex(col: str, kind: str):
    key = (col, kind)
    if key not in _CACHE:
        esc = re.escape(col)
        alts = [rf'''\\?["'`]{esc}\\?["'`]''', rf"[.$@]{esc}(?![A-Za-z0-9_])"]
        if kind == "ruby":
            alts.append(rf"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_:]):{esc}(?![A-Za-z0-9_])")
        if kind == "sql":
            alts.append(rf"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_]){esc}(?![A-Za-z0-9_])")
        for v in _variants(col):
            alts.append(rf"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])(?:get|set)?{re.escape(v)}(?![A-Za-z0-9_])")
        _CACHE[key] = re.compile("|".join(alts), re.I if kind == "sql" else 0)
    return _CACHE[key]


def _find_columns(text: str, columns: Iterable[str], kind: str) -> Dict[str, List[int]]:
    starts = [0] + [m.end() for m in re.finditer("\n", text)]
    hits: Dict[str, List[int]] = {}
    for col in columns:
        lines: List[int] = []
        for m in _column_regex(col, kind).finditer(text):
            ln = bisect.bisect_right(starts, m.start())
            if ln not in lines:
                lines.append(ln)
            if len(lines) >= MAX_LINES:
                break
        if lines:
            hits[col] = lines
    return hits


def _reads(text: str, language: str, names: Sequence[str], stems: Sequence[str]) -> str:
    if any(n in text for n in names):
        return "file name"
    if language == "SQL" and any(
            re.search(rf"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_]){re.escape(st)}(?![A-Za-z0-9_])", text, re.I) for st in stems):
        return "table name"
    return ""


def scan_repo(root, data_file, columns: Sequence[str], exclude: Iterable[str] = (),
              scope: str = "readers", also_names: Sequence[str] = ()) -> ScanResult:
    """Search `root` for code that reads `data_file` (or any file named in `also_names`, e.g. the file
    the baseline was built from, which may differ from the file being checked)."""
    root = Path(root).resolve()
    names, stems = [], []
    for cand in (str(data_file), *also_names):
        c = Path(cand)
        try:
            rel = c.resolve().relative_to(root).as_posix()
        except ValueError:
            rel = c.name
        names += [c.name, rel]
        stems.append(c.stem)
    names, stems = list(dict.fromkeys(names)), list(dict.fromkeys(stems))
    skip = DEFAULT_EXCLUDE | set(exclude)
    ecosystems, langs, refs = set(), Counter(), []
    scanned, truncated = 0, False
    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
        dirnames[:] = sorted(d for d in dirnames if d not in skip and not d.startswith("."))
        for fn in sorted(filenames):
            if fn in MANIFESTS:
                ecosystems.add(MANIFESTS[fn])
            elif fn.endswith((".csproj", ".sln")):
                ecosystems.add(".NET (C#)")
            elif fn.endswith(".Rproj"):
                ecosystems.add("R")
            language = LANGUAGES.get(Path(fn).suffix.lower())
            if not language:
                continue
            path = Path(dirpath) / fn
            try:
                if path.stat().st_size > MAX_BYTES:
                    continue
                if scanned >= MAX_FILES:
                    truncated = True
                    continue
                text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
            except OSError:
                continue
            scanned += 1
            langs[language] += 1
            via = _reads(text, language, names, stems)
            if scope == "readers" and not via:
                continue
            kind = "sql" if language == "SQL" else "ruby" if language == "Ruby" else "other"
            hits = _find_columns(text, columns, kind)
            if via or hits:
                refs.append(CodeRef(path.relative_to(root).as_posix(), language, bool(via),
                                    via or "column text match", hits))
    return ScanResult(str(root), sorted(ecosystems), dict(langs.most_common()), scanned, truncated, refs)


def describe_scan(scan: ScanResult) -> str:
    langs = ", ".join(f"{n} {l}" for l, n in scan.languages.items()) or "no recognized source files"
    eco = f"; ecosystems: {', '.join(scan.ecosystems)}" if scan.ecosystems else ""
    more = " (stopped at the file limit)" if scan.truncated else ""
    return f"scanned {scan.files_scanned} source files ({langs}){eco}{more}"


def attach_code_references(report, scan: ScanResult, max_files: int = 8) -> None:
    """Add detected code references to each issue (a separate, labelled, heuristic section)."""
    for issue in report.issues:
        found = []
        for ref in scan.refs:
            if issue.columns:
                cols = {c: ls for c, ls in ref.columns.items() if c in issue.columns}
                if cols:
                    found.append({"file": ref.file, "language": ref.language, "via": ref.via, "columns": cols})
            elif ref.reads_dataset:
                found.append({"file": ref.file, "language": ref.language, "via": ref.via, "columns": {}})
        issue.code_refs = found[:max_files]
        issue.code_refs_more = max(0, len(found) - max_files)


def consumers_yaml(scan: ScanResult) -> str:
    import yaml
    consumers = {
        r.file: {"type": f"{language_slug(r.language)}-code",
                 "description": f"Detected by `datapulse scan` ({r.via}); heuristic, please review",
                 "columns": sorted(r.columns)}
        for r in scan.refs if r.reads_dataset and r.columns
    }
    head = ("# Consumers detected by `datapulse scan` (heuristic text search; review before use).\n"
            "# Merge the entries you trust into the `consumers:` section of datapulse.yml.\n"
            "# Nothing has been applied.\nversion: 1\n")
    return head + (yaml.safe_dump({"consumers": consumers}, sort_keys=False) if consumers else "consumers: {}\n")

"""Propose validation rules from a profile. Suggestions are only ever printed or
written to a separate file; the user's datapulse.yml is never modified."""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Dict, List

import yaml


@dataclass
class Suggestion:
    section: str
    rationale: str
    fragment: Dict[str, Any]


def suggest_rules(profile: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[Suggestion]:
    cols, rows = profile["columns"], profile["rows"]
    out: List[Suggestion] = []

    out.append(Suggestion(
        "schema", f"The baseline has {len(cols)} columns; flag dropped columns and type changes as critical.",
        {"missing_columns": "critical", "added_columns": "warning", "type_changes": "critical"}))

    out.append(Suggestion(
        "row_count", f"The baseline has {rows:,} rows; flag swings of more than 20%.",
        {"severity": "warning", "max_change_pct": 20}))

    never_null = [c for c, i in cols.items() if i["null_count"] == 0]
    if never_null and rows:
        out.append(Suggestion(
            "null_rate", f"{len(never_null)} column(s) have no nulls in the baseline, so any null is a regression: "
                         f"{', '.join(never_null)}.",
            {"severity": "critical", "max_increase": 0.05, "columns": {c: 0.0 for c in never_null}}))
    elif rows:
        out.append(Suggestion("null_rate", "Every column has some nulls; allow a 5 point increase.",
                              {"severity": "critical", "max_increase": 0.05}))

    unique = [c for c, i in cols.items() if rows >= 2 and i["null_count"] == 0 and i["unique_count"] == rows]
    id_like = [c for c in unique if "id" in c.lower()]
    key = id_like[:1] or (unique[:1] if len(unique) == 1 else [])
    if key:
        out.append(Suggestion("duplicates", f"'{key[0]}' is unique and never null across all {rows:,} rows; "
                                            "treat it as a key.",
                              {"severity": "critical", "max_duplicate_rate": 0.0, "columns": key}))

    categorical = [c for c, i in cols.items() if "categories" in i]
    if categorical:
        detail = "; ".join(f"{c}: {len(cols[c]['categories'])} values" for c in categorical)
        out.append(Suggestion("categorical", f"Low-cardinality text columns where new values deserve a look "
                                             f"({detail}).",
                              {"severity": "warning", "max_unexpected_rate": 0.0, "columns": categorical}))

    numeric = [c for c, i in cols.items() if i.get("numeric") and i["numeric"]["std"] > 0]
    if numeric:
        out.append(Suggestion("distribution", f"Numeric columns with spread in the baseline: {', '.join(numeric)}.",
                              {"severity": "warning", "psi_threshold": 0.2, "max_mean_shift_std": 1.0,
                               "columns": numeric}))
    return out


def render_yaml(suggestions: List[Suggestion]) -> str:
    lines = [
        "# Rules suggested by `datapulse suggest`. Review each block, then copy what you want",
        "# into the `checks:` section of datapulse.yml. Nothing here has been applied.",
        "version: 1", "checks:",
    ]
    for s in suggestions:
        lines.append(f"  # {s.rationale}")
        block = yaml.safe_dump({s.section: s.fragment}, sort_keys=False, default_flow_style=None).rstrip()
        lines.extend("  " + ln for ln in block.splitlines())
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"

FILE [84/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\src\datapulse\unify.py

"""Unify: bring every source into the project's standard format, then combine them into one table.

The standard format is the project's baseline (its columns, types and value scales). Mapping a source produces a
recipe built from the repair agent's safe operations (rename, scale, convert, standardize, de-duplicate, drop):
1. columns whose names match apart from case and punctuation are mapped without AI;
2. the AI proposes the rest from statistics only (the same agent loop as repairs, in "map" mode);
3. a person approves the recipe once;
4. every later pull replays it with no AI, so loads are fast, free and predictable. A step that no longer applies
   (the source changed shape) is reported, never guessed around, and the checks flag what's left.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import re
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import pandas as pd

from .ai import get_api_key
from .config import Config
from .fix import OPERATIONS, RepairError, RepairSession, _counts, apply_operations, run_repair

SOURCE_COLUMN = "_source"


def _key(name: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", name.lower())


def name_matches(columns: List[str], standard: List[str]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Renames for columns that equal a standard column apart from case, spaces and punctuation."""
    by_key: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
    for s in standard:
        if s not in columns:
            by_key.setdefault(_key(s), []).append(s)
    claimed, ops = set(), []
    for c in columns:
        targets = by_key.get(_key(c), [])
        if c not in standard and len(targets) == 1 and targets[0] not in claimed:
            claimed.add(targets[0])
            ops.append({"operation": "rename_column", "args": {"column": c, "new_name": targets[0]},
                        "reason": "Same name as the standard column apart from capitalisation or punctuation."})
    return ops


def apply_recipe(df: pd.DataFrame, baseline_profile: Dict[str, Any], recipe: List[Dict[str, Any]]
                 ) -> Tuple[pd.DataFrame, List[str], List[str]]:
    """Replay a recipe; steps that no longer apply are skipped and reported. Returns (frame, effects, problems)."""
    effects, problems = [], []
    for n, op in enumerate(recipe, 1):
        try:
            df, effect = OPERATIONS[op["operation"]](df, baseline_profile, **op["args"])
            effects.append(effect)
        except (RepairError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
            problems.append(f"Step {n} ({op['operation'].replace('_', ' ')}) no longer applies: {e}")
    return df, effects, problems


def validate_recipe(recipe: Any) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """A recipe sent back by the browser after review: known operations with plain arguments only."""
    if not isinstance(recipe, list) or len(recipe) > 100:
        raise ValueError("A recipe is a list of at most 100 steps.")
    clean = []
    for step in recipe:
        if not isinstance(step, dict) or step.get("operation") not in OPERATIONS or not isinstance(step.get("args"), dict):
            raise ValueError("Each step needs a known operation and its arguments.")
        args = step["args"]
        if not all(isinstance(k, str) and isinstance(v, (str, int, float, list)) for k, v in args.items()):
            raise ValueError("Step arguments must be plain values.")
        clean.append({"operation": step["operation"], "args": args, "reason": str(step.get("reason", ""))[:500]})
    return clean


def propose_mapping(df: pd.DataFrame, baseline: Dict[str, Any], cfg: Config, client=None,
                    reference: Optional[pd.DataFrame] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """A proposed recipe for one source, with before/after issue counts. Nothing is saved."""
    standard = list(baseline["profile"]["columns"])
    session = RepairSession(df, baseline, cfg, reference=reference, operations=name_matches(list(df.columns), standard))
    automatic = len(session.operations)
    if (client or get_api_key(cfg)) and session.check().issues:
        result = run_repair(session, client=client, mode="map")
        result.pop("fixed_frame")
    else:
        mapped, effects = apply_operations(df, baseline["profile"], session.operations)
        after = session.check(mapped)
        result = {
            "summary": f"Matched {automatic} column(s) by name."
                       + (" AI is off, so nothing else was mapped." if after.issues else ""),
            "not_fixed": [], "warnings": [],
            "operations": [{**op, "effect": e} for op, e in zip(session.operations, effects)],
            "before": _counts(session.check(session.original).issues),
            "after": {**_counts(after.issues), "remaining": [i.title for i in after.issues]},
            "rows": {"before": len(df), "after": len(mapped)}, "model": None,
        }
    result["automatic_steps"] = automatic
    result["source_columns"] = list(df.columns)
    return result


def combine(frames: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame], standard: List[str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Stack mapped sources into one table in the standard's column order, tagging each row with its source.

    Columns a source doesn't have are left empty (the checks will flag them); extra columns are dropped.
    """
    parts = []
    for name, df in frames.items():
        part = df.reindex(columns=standard)
        part.insert(0, SOURCE_COLUMN, name)
        parts.append(part)
    return pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True) if parts else pd.DataFrame(columns=[SOURCE_COLUMN, *standard])

"""Turn any supported file into a table.

Spreadsheets and JSON stay tables. A PDF, Word, text or HTML file that contains a table (one row of the data per
line) is read back as that table. Other documents, email, images and zip archives go through the document unifier:
with an AI key, records are extracted; without one, each document is one row of its text.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from datapulse.config import Config
from datapulse.errors import DataPulseError
from datapulse.io import ARCHIVES, DOCUMENTS, INPUTS, load_dataframe
from datapulse.unifier.documents import expand_zip, is_document, is_tabular
from datapulse.unifier.pipeline import unify_files

from .base import SourceError
from .tabular_text import document_frame


def frame_from_path(path, label: str | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """A file on disk as a DataFrame. `label` is the name shown on document rows."""
    path = Path(path)
    label = label or path.name
    ext = path.suffix.lower()
    if ext in ARCHIVES:
        dest = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())
        files, _skipped = expand_zip(path, dest, label)
        return _rows(files, label)
    if is_tabular(path):
        return load_dataframe(path)
    if is_document(path):
        table = document_frame(path, label)
        if table is not None:
            return table
        return _rows([(path, label)], label)
    raise SourceError(
        f"Unsupported file type '{path.suffix or 'unknown'}'. "
        "Use a spreadsheet (CSV, Excel, JSON, Parquet) or a document (PDF, Word, email, text, HTML, image, zip)."
    )


def frame_from_bytes(data: bytes, name: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Bytes plus a file name (the extension decides how to read them)."""
    ext = Path(name).suffix.lower()
    if ext not in INPUTS:
        raise SourceError(f"Unsupported file type '{ext or 'unknown'}' for {name}")
    dest = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())
    path = dest / f"download{ext}"
    path.write_bytes(data)
    return frame_from_path(path, name)


def _rows(files, label: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    try:
        result = unify_files(files, None, Config(), source_label=label)
    except DataPulseError as exc:
        raise SourceError(str(exc)) from exc
    if not result.rows:
        raise SourceError(f"No records could be read from {label}")
    return pd.DataFrame(result.rows)

FILE [88/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\src\datapulse\sources\base.py

"""The plug-in contract: every data source, database or not, answers one question - "give me your data as a table".

A plug-in is a class with a `kind`, the `fields` its setup form asks for, a `test()` that proves the connection
works, and a `fetch()` that returns a pandas DataFrame. Decorate it with `@register` and put it in this package;
it then appears in the dashboard's "Add source" menu automatically. Everything after `fetch()` (mapping to the
project's standard format, checks, AI) is shared, so a plug-in never deals with any of it.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import os
import re
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
from typing import Any, ClassVar, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
from urllib.parse import unquote, urlsplit

import pandas as pd

from ..errors import DataPulseError
from ..io import normalize_frame


class SourceError(DataPulseError):
    """A source that can't be reached or read; the message is shown to the user."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Field:
    name: str
    label: str
    placeholder: str = ""
    secret: bool = False      # stored on the server only; never sent back to the browser
    required: bool = True
    multiline: bool = False
    help: str = ""
    input: str = "text"       # "text", or "file" for an uploaded file (the server stores it and keeps its id)


class Source:
    kind: ClassVar[str]                 # unique id, e.g. "postgres"
    label: ClassVar[str]                # shown in the menu, e.g. "PostgreSQL"
    group: ClassVar[str]                # menu section: "Databases", "Files & links", "Apps"
    description: ClassVar[str] = ""
    fields: ClassVar[Tuple[Field, ...]] = ()
    requires: ClassVar[Tuple[str, ...]] = ()  # Python packages the plug-in imports
    hidden: ClassVar[bool] = False            # still works for saved sources, but not offered in the menu
    has_sample: ClassVar[bool] = False        # the Add source form can fill itself with test data

    def test(self, settings: Dict[str, str]) -> str:
        """Connect and return a short success message, e.g. "Connected. 12 tables."; raise SourceError if not."""
        raise NotImplementedError

    def fetch(self, settings: Dict[str, str], max_rows: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Return the data as a table. Fetch at most max_rows + 1 rows so the caller can tell it was too big."""
        raise NotImplementedError


REGISTRY: Dict[str, Source] = {}


def register(cls):
    if cls.kind in REGISTRY:
        raise ValueError(f"Two data-source plug-ins use the kind '{cls.kind}'")
    REGISTRY[cls.kind] = cls()
    return cls


def max_rows() -> int:
    return int(os.getenv("DATAPULSE_MAX_SOURCE_ROWS", "200000"))


def _missing(plugin: Source) -> List[str]:
    return [p for p in plugin.requires if importlib.util.find_spec(p) is None]


GROUPS = ("Databases", "Files & links", "Apps")


def error_text(e: Exception) -> str:
    """A driver exception as one readable line, without SQLAlchemy's "(psycopg.OperationalError) " prefix."""
    first_line = (str(e).strip().splitlines() or [type(e).__name__])[0]
    return re.sub(r"^\([\w.]+\)\s*", "", first_line)


def catalog() -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Every plug-in and the form it needs, for the "Add source" menu."""
    plugins = sorted(REGISTRY.values(), key=lambda p: GROUPS.index(p.group) if p.group in GROUPS else len(GROUPS))
    return [{"kind": p.kind, "label": p.label, "group": p.group, "description": p.description,
             "fields": [asdict(f) for f in p.fields], "available": not _missing(p),
             "missing_packages": _missing(p), "hidden": p.hidden, "has_sample": p.has_sample} for p in plugins]


def plugin(kind: str) -> Source:
    if kind not in REGISTRY:
        raise SourceError(f"Unknown source type '{kind}'.")
    found = REGISTRY[kind]
    if _missing(found):
        raise SourceError(f"{found.label} needs the Python package(s) {', '.join(_missing(found))} on the server. "
                          "Install them with: pip install 'datapulse[sources]'")
    return found


def clean_settings(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, Any], previous: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None) -> Dict[str, str]:
    """Validate a setup form. A blank secret keeps the saved one, so editing a source never needs the password again."""
    fields = {f.name: f for f in plugin(kind).fields}
    unknown = set(settings) - set(fields)
    if unknown:
        raise SourceError(f"Unknown setting(s) for {REGISTRY[kind].label}: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}")
    out = {}
    for name, f in fields.items():
        value = str(settings.get(name) or "").strip()
        if not value and f.secret and previous:
            value = previous.get(name, "")
        if f.required and not value:
            raise SourceError(f"{f.label} is required.")
        if value:
            out[name] = value
    return out


def public_settings(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, str]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Settings safe to show in the browser: secrets are replaced by whether one is saved."""
    secret = {f.name for f in REGISTRY[kind].fields if f.secret} if kind in REGISTRY else set(settings)
    return {k: ("" if k in secret else v) for k, v in settings.items()} | {
        "saved_secrets": sorted(k for k in settings if k in secret)}


def _secrets(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, str]) -> List[str]:
    """Secret values, plus the password inside any connection URL, so error messages can be scrubbed."""
    found = []
    for f in REGISTRY[kind].fields:
        value = settings.get(f.name)
        if f.secret and value:
            found.append(value)
            try:
                password = urlsplit(value).password
            except ValueError:
                password = None
            if password:
                found += [password, unquote(password)]
    return sorted(set(found), key=len, reverse=True)


def _scrub(message: str, secrets: List[str]) -> str:
    for s in secrets:
        if len(s) >= 3:
            message = message.replace(s, "•••")
    return re.sub(r"(://[^:/@\s]+:)[^@\s]+@", r"\1•••@", message)  # any other user:password@ in a URL


def _guard(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, str], action):
    try:
        return action()
    except SourceError as e:
        raise SourceError(_scrub(str(e), _secrets(kind, settings))) from None
    except Exception as e:  # drivers raise their own exception types; show their message, never a password
        raise SourceError(f"{REGISTRY[kind].label}: {_scrub(error_text(e), _secrets(kind, settings))}") from None


def try_connection(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, str]) -> str:
    found = plugin(kind)
    return _guard(kind, settings, lambda: found.test(settings))


def fetch_source(kind: str, settings: Dict[str, str], name: str = "source") -> pd.DataFrame:
    found, limit = plugin(kind), max_rows()
    df = _guard(kind, settings, lambda: found.fetch(settings, limit))
    if len(df) > limit:
        raise SourceError(f"{name} returned more than {limit:,} rows. Narrow the query or collection filter "
                          "(DATAPULSE_MAX_SOURCE_ROWS raises the limit).")
    if len(df.columns) == 0:
        raise SourceError(f"{name} returned no columns.")
    return normalize_frame(df.reset_index(drop=True), name)


def flatten(records: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Nested documents (MongoDB, JSON APIs) as flat columns: {"address": {"city": "X"}} -> "address.city"."""
    if not records:
        return pd.DataFrame()
    return pd.json_normalize(records, sep=".")

FILE [89/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\src\datapulse\sources\connected.py

"""Reading downloaded or uploaded files into tables, shared by the File and Link sources.

Files become tables here: data files (CSV, Excel, JSON, Parquet) are read directly, JSON records keep
their nesting as flat columns, and documents (PDF, Word, text, email, HTML, images) go through the AI unifier's
extract path, which pulls structured records out of them (one record of text per document without an API key).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from ..config import Config
from ..io import JSON_LINES, load_dataframe
from .base import SourceError, flatten


def read_any(path: Path, label: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """A downloaded or uploaded file as a table."""
    from ..unifier import unify_files
    from ..unifier.documents import is_document, is_tabular
    from ..unifier.records import read_raw_records

    ext = path.suffix.lower()
    if ext in (".json", *JSON_LINES):
        return flatten(read_raw_records(path, label))  # API-style records, nesting kept as address.city columns
    if is_tabular(path):
        return load_dataframe(path)
    if is_document(path):
        from .tabular_text import document_frame

        table = document_frame(path, label)
        if table is not None:
            return table
        result = unify_files([(path, label)], None, Config(), source_label=label)
        if not result.rows:
            raise SourceError(f"No records could be extracted from {label}.")
        return pd.DataFrame(result.rows)
    raise SourceError(f"{label}: unsupported file type '{ext or 'unknown'}'.")


def download(connector, config: dict, secrets: dict, label: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Run one of the unifier's connectors into a temporary folder and read what it saved."""
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="datapulse-") as folder:
        return read_any(connector.fetch(config, secrets, Path(folder)), label)

FILE [90/142]: D:\my_git\folder_extractor\Datapulse-main (1)\Datapulse-main\src\datapulse\sources\database.py
